\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}):=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\tilde{f}_{n,\varepsilon}H_{n}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon},\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
. Choosing
n=4-m
in
eq. (
3.10
) we obtain

\Phi_{\zeta}^{[m]}=\frac{3!}{(3-m)!}f_{3,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{3-m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta}^{[m]}

(3.11)

and a remainder with an infinite chaos decomposition strictly greater that
3-m
:

\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}=\delta^{4-m}(G^{[4-m]}_{[1]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h_{\zeta}^{\otimes 4-m}).

(3.12)

This is a key step in the proof of Theorem
3.1
. Indeed, it
suffices to substitute (
3.11
) into
definition (
2.2
) to identify the remainder

\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}
in decomposition (
3.5
) that
has to converge to zero, and see that it always contains the term

\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}
. Moreover, the
structure (
3.12
) of
\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}

makes it possible to bound its
L^{p}
norm and
obtain (
3.7
), (
3.8
) in the same way as done
in Section
3.1
for
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}
. We will consider
separately
simple trees
(namely
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}},\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}
) which are linear functions of
\Phi^{[m]}
in
Section
3.3
, and
composite trees
(namely

Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}},\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}
) which are
quadratic in simple trees and need to be further renormalized in order to
converge to some limit as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. We will show the
decomposition (
3.5
) for composite trees in
Section
3.4.1
and the
bounds (
3.7
), (
3.8
) in
Section
3.4.2
.

Remark 3.2
.

We can easily estimate terms of the form

\varepsilon^{-(m-3)/2}\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta}
for
3\leqslant m\leqslant 9
and every
p\in[2,\infty)
. We have (as already observed for

F^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}
):

\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{m-3}{2}}\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta}\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}}=\|F^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{1/2}Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta})\|^{p}_{L^{p}}=\int_{\mathbbm{R}}|F^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}(x)|^{p}\gamma(\mathrm{d}x)

where
\gamma(\mathrm{d}x)
is the density of a centered Gaussian with
variance
\sigma^{2}_{\varepsilon}
. The integral is finite by
Assumption
1
: in particular we only need to assume that the first

m
derivatives of
F_{\varepsilon}
have exponential growth (actually, it
is easy to see that one can require even weaker growth conditions).

3.3
Analysis of simple trees

First of all note that the term
\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}
has no
remainder, and then it can be shown to converge in law to
\lambda^{(2)}Y^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}
by usual techniques (see
[
4
]
). In
this section we show the convergence of the trees
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing}
,

Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}
,
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}
,

Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}
. We obtain easily
from (
3.11
):

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\tau}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{(3-m)!}{3!}\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}

(3.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{3,\varepsilon}\int_{\zeta}\llbracket Y^{(3-m)}_{\varepsilon,\zeta}\rrbracket\mu_{\zeta}+\frac{(3-m)!}{3!}\int_{\zeta}\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\tau}(\lambda_{\varepsilon})\Delta_{q}K^{\tau}(Y_{\varepsilon})(t,x)+\Delta_{q}\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}(t,x),

with
\zeta=(s,y)
and either

\begin{array}[]{llllll}\mu_{q,\zeta}&=&\delta(t-s)K_{q,x}(y)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}y,&&\text{for}&\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing},\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}},\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}},\\
\mu_{q,\zeta}&=&\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}y\int K_{q,x}(z)P_{t-s}(z-y)\mathrm{d}z,&&\text{for}&\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}},\end{array}

(3.14)

where
K_{q,x}(y)
is the kernel associated to the Littlewood-Paley block

\Delta_{q}
and
P_{t}(x)
is the heat kernel.

As said before,
f_{3,\varepsilon}\int_{\zeta}\llbracket Y^{(3-m)}_{\varepsilon,\zeta}\rrbracket\mu_{q,\zeta}
converges in law in
L^{p}

for every
2\leqslant p\char 60\relax+\infty
to
\lambda_{3}\int_{\zeta}\llbracket Y^{(3-m)}_{\zeta}\rrbracket\mu_{q,\zeta}
since
f_{3,\varepsilon}\rightarrow\lambda_{3}
by Assumption
1
. We can bound the remainder
term
\int_{\zeta}\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}
in
L^{p}(\Omega)

using Lemma
B.2
and the definition of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbbm{D}^{4-m,p}(H^{\otimes 4-m})}
to obtain:

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\zeta}\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\delta^{4-m}G^{[4-m]}_{[1]}\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|G^{[4-m]}_{[1]}\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{\mathbbm{D}^{4-m,p}(H^{\otimes 4-m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{4-m}\left\|\mathrm{D}^{k}G^{[4-m]}_{[1]}\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,H^{\otimes 4-m+k})}.

From Corollary
B.6
we know that
(j-L)^{-1}
and
\mathrm{D}(j-L)^{-1}
are bounded in
L^{p}
for every
p\in[2,\infty)
and every
j\geqslant 1
. Applying repeatedly these estimations we obtain:

\left\|\mathrm{D}^{k}G^{[4-m]}_{[1]}\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,H^{\otimes 4-m+k})}\lesssim\left\|\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,H^{\otimes 4-m})}.

Now we can proceed to implement the idea we already described in
Section
3.1
, i.e. estimating out the term
\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}
(which is bounded by
Remark
3.2
but with infinite chaos decomposition) and
considering the finite-chaos term that is left. We do this by decomposing the

L^{p}(\Omega,H^{\otimes 4-m})
norm as norms on
H^{\otimes 4-m}
and

L^{p/2}(\Omega)
as follows:

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,H^{\otimes 4-m})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\left\|\int_{\zeta}\Phi_{\zeta}^{[4]}h_{\zeta}^{\otimes 4-m}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|^{2}_{H^{\otimes 4-m}}\right\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\zeta}\Phi_{\zeta}^{[4]}\Phi_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{[4]}\langle h_{\zeta}^{\otimes 4-m},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{\otimes 4-m}\rangle_{H^{\otimes 4-m}}\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\|\Phi_{\zeta}^{[4]}\Phi_{\zeta^{\prime}}^{[4]}\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}|\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle|^{4-m}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{1/2}.

Finally, putting the estimations together and using Hölder’s inequality,
together with the bound
\varepsilon|\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle|=\varepsilon|C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta-\zeta^{\prime})|\lesssim 1
of
Lemma
A.12
, we obtain for every
\delta\in(0,1]
:

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\zeta}\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\varepsilon\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}|\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle|^{4-m}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\varepsilon^{\delta}\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}|\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle|^{3-m+\delta}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now using Remark
3.2
(note that to bound
\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{[4]}
we only need to control the first 4 derivatives of

F_{\varepsilon}
) and the fact that
\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle_{H}=C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta-\zeta^{\prime})
we obtain as a final estimation

\left\|\int_{\zeta}\hat{\Phi}^{(m)}_{\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}\left[\int|C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta-\zeta^{\prime})|^{3-m+\delta}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{1/2}.

(3.15)

From the definition (
3.14
) of the measure
\mu_{q,\zeta}
, the l.h.s of (
3.15
) can be estimated in a
standard way using Lemma
A.14
to obtain for every

x\in\mathbbm{T}^{3}
,
q>0
:

\begin{array}[]{lllllll}\left\|\Delta_{q}\hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}(t,x)\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}2^{-\frac{1-\delta}{2}q}&&\left\|\Delta_{q}\hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}(t,x)\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}2^{\frac{1+\delta}{2}q},\\
\left\|\Delta_{q}\hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}(t,x)\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}2^{\frac{2+\delta}{2}q}&&\left\|\Delta_{q}\hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing}(t,x)\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}2^{\frac{\delta}{2}q}.\end{array}

Time regularity of trees

We want to show (
3.8
). In order to do that, we compute in the
same way as before:

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\zeta}(\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{t,x}-\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{s,x})\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\delta^{4-m}\int_{\zeta}G^{[4-m]}_{[1]}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}h^{\otimes 4-m}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x}h_{s,x}^{\otimes 4-m})\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\left\|\int_{\zeta}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})h_{s,x}^{\otimes 4-m}\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|^{2}_{H^{\otimes 4-m}}\right\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\left\|\int_{\zeta}\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x}(h_{t,x}^{\otimes 4-m}-h_{s,x}^{\otimes 4-m})\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|^{2}_{H^{\otimes 4-m}}\right\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}.

We focus on the first term above to obtain that it is bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\zeta}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x^{\prime}}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x^{\prime}})\langle h_{s,x}^{\otimes 4-m},h_{s,x^{\prime}}^{\otimes 4-m}\rangle_{H^{\otimes 4-m}}\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\|(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x^{\prime}}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x^{\prime}})\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}|\langle h_{s,x},h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle|^{4-m}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\varepsilon\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\|\varepsilon^{-1}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x^{\prime}}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x^{\prime}})\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}|\langle h_{s,x},h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle|^{4-m}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\varepsilon^{\delta}\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}\|\varepsilon^{-1}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x^{\prime}}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x^{\prime}})\|_{L^{p/2}(\Omega)}|\langle h_{s,x},h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle|^{3-m+\delta}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now note that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{(4)}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x))-F^{(4)}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{1}F^{(5)}[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x)+\tau\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x))](Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x)),

and we can estimate
\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Phi^{[4]}_{t,x}-\Phi^{[4]}_{s,x})\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}
by hypercontractivity and using
Lemma
A.15
as

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{1/2}\left\|\int_{0}^{1}F^{(5)}[\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x)+\tau\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x))]\right\|_{L^{2p}(\Omega)}\|Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{1/2}[C_{\varepsilon}(0,0)-C_{\varepsilon}(t-s,0)]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-2\sigma}|t-s|^{\sigma}

for any
\sigma\in[0,1/2]
. The other term can be estimated more easily
by

\displaystyle\left[\varepsilon^{\delta}\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}|\langle h_{s,x},h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle|^{2-m+\delta}|\langle h_{t,x}-h_{s,x},h_{t,x^{\prime}}-h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle||\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-2\kappa}|t-s|^{\sigma}\left[\varepsilon^{\delta}\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}|\langle h_{s,x},h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle|^{3-m+\delta+2\sigma}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}},

and finally obtain

\left\|\int_{\zeta}(\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{t,x}-\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{s,x})\mu_{q,\zeta}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta/2-2\kappa}|t-s|^{\sigma}\left[\int_{\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}}|\langle h_{s,x},h_{s,x^{\prime}}\rangle|^{3-m+\delta+2\sigma}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Which yields estimation (
3.8
) by applying
Lemma
A.14
as before. This concludes the treatment
of simple trees. Notice that in this section we only needed
F_{\varepsilon}\in C^{5}(\mathbbm{R})
with the first 5 derivatives having exponential growth:
indeed we need to take 4 derivatives to bound
\varepsilon^{1/2}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}
as of Remark
3.2
, plus one more
derivative for the time regularity of
\varepsilon^{1/2}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}
.

3.4
Analysis of composite trees

In this section we show the decomposition (
3.5
) and the
bound (
3.7
) for the trees
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}},\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}
. The time regularity (
3.8
) of

\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}
can be obtained with the same technique as in
the previous section, assuming that we can control one more derivative of

F_{\varepsilon}
than what is needed to prove the boundedness of

Y^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}
(thus we will need
F_{\varepsilon}\in C^{9}(\mathbbm{R})
with exponential growth, as discussed in
Remark
3.7
). Looking at the definitions in (
2.2
)
it is clear that we can write the
q
-th Littlewood-Paley blocks of

Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}
,
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}
,

\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}
and
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}

\forall\varepsilon>0
as:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{6}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}-d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta}),\\
\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{9}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}-d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta}),\\
\Delta_{q}\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}-\bar{d}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta}),\\
\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}-d_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(\bar{\zeta})-d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta}),\end{array}

(3.16)

for any time-space point
\bar{\zeta}=(t,\bar{x})
that we keep fixed
throughout this section. In order to keep the notation shorter we defined

\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}:=\varepsilon^{-1/2}f_{2,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1})\rrbracket,

which can be thought of as a finite-chaos equivalent of

\Phi^{[1]}_{\varepsilon}
(modulo a constant
f_{2,\varepsilon}/f_{3,\varepsilon}
) in the same way as
\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}
is a
finite-chaos equivalent of
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}
. The measure
\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}
on
(\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3})^{2}
is given by

\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}:=[\int_{x,y}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(y-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2},

with
\zeta_{i:=}(s_{i},x_{i})
for
i=1,2
,
K
being the kernel
associated to the Littlewood-Paley decomposition and
P
being the heat
kernel. The first step for decomposing (
3.16
) is to
expand them using the partial chaos expansion (
B.3
) to obtain

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}&=&\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}),\\
\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}&=&\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}),\\
\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi_{\zeta_{2}}^{[1]}&=&\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+\delta[J_{0}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})]+\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\mathrm{D}^{2}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\\
&=&\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{2})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\mathrm{D}^{2}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}).\end{array}

(3.17)

Like the trees appearing in the
\Phi^{4}_{3}
model, we expect composite trees to
require a further renormalisation, on top of the Wick ordering. We
developed (
3.17
) to the smallest order that allows us to see
the effect of renormalization.

3.4.1
Renormalisation of composite trees

In this section we show how to renormalize (
3.16
) by
estimating terms of the type
\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]
in expansion (
3.17
). This poses an
additional difficulty, as in principle we would need to compute an infinite
number of contractions between
\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}
and

\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}
. However, we can again decompose
\Phi^{[m]}
as
in (
3.11
), and then the product
formula (
B.6
) ensures that we only need to control a
finite number of contractions. This is another important step in the proof and
will be carried out in Lemma
3.5
. First we need some
preparatory results:

Lemma 3.3
.

We have

\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=\int_{s,x}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{x}-x)G(t-s,x).

and

\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{2})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=\int_{x}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x}-x)H(t,x),

where we introduced the kernels:

\displaystyle G(t-s,x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s}(x_{1}^{\prime})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(t-s,x_{2})}],

\displaystyle H(t,x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{s,x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)P_{t-s}(x_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\Phi^{[2]}_{(t-s,-x_{1})}].

Remark 3.4
.

Some caveat on the notation: although we use the same letter for the kernel

G(\cdot,\cdot)
and the Green operator
G_{[m]}^{[n]}
, those two are
not related in any possible way. It is always clear which one the notation
refers to.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s_{1},x_{1},x_{2},x,x_{1}^{\prime}}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}(s_{1},x_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(t-s_{1},x_{2}-x_{1})}]

and by change of variables, exploiting the translation invariance of the
problem we obtain:

=\int_{s_{1},x_{1},x}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x+x_{1})Y(s_{1},x_{1})\int_{x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(t-s_{1},x_{2})}].

Using the definition of
K_{q}
we have

=\int_{s_{1},x}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(s_{1},\bar{x}-x)\int_{x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(t-s_{1},x_{2})}].

Finally we can write

\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=\int_{s_{1},x}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(s_{1},\bar{x}-x)G(t-s_{1},x).

Similar computations holds for the other term, indeed

\displaystyle\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{2})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s_{1},x_{1},x_{2},x,x_{1}^{\prime}}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x_{2})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\Phi^{[2]}_{(t-s_{1},x_{2}-x_{1})}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{x_{2}}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x+x_{2})Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x_{2})\int_{s_{1,}x_{1},x,x_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\Phi^{[2]}_{(t-s_{1},-x_{1})}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{x}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x}-x)\int_{s_{1,}x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\Phi^{[2]}_{(t-s_{1},-x_{1})}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{x}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x}-x)H(t,x)

Substituting the lemma above and (
3.17
) in the
expressions (
3.16
), we can write them as:

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{9}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta})\left[\frac{1}{9}\int_{s,x}G(t-s,x)-d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\right]

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta})\left[\frac{1}{3}\int_{s,x}\bar{G}(t-s,x)-\bar{d}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\right]

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta})\left[\frac{1}{6}\int_{x}H(t,x)-d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}\right]

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta^{2}G_{1}^{2}\mathrm{D}^{2}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\Delta_{q}(1)(\bar{\zeta})\left[\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}-d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\right]

\displaystyle+\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(\bar{\zeta})\left[\frac{1}{3}\int_{s,x}G(t-s,x)+\frac{1}{3}\int_{x}H(t,x)-d_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

with the additional definitions

\displaystyle\bar{G}(t-s,x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1})\mathbbm{E}[\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(t-s,-x_{1})}],

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]G(t-s,x),

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]H(t,x).

Now we can characterise the local behaviour of
\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]
appearing in the integrals above.
Decomposing separately
\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}
and
\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}
as
in (
3.11
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3!^{2}}{(3-m)!(3-n)!}(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{3-m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y^{3-n}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}\rrbracket]+\frac{3!}{(3-m)!}f_{3,\varepsilon}\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{3-m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{2}}^{[n]}]

\displaystyle+\frac{3!}{(3-n)!}f_{3,\varepsilon}\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{3-n}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{1}}^{[m]}]+\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{1}}^{[m]}\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{2}}^{[n]}],

where
\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{3-m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y^{3-n}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}\rrbracket]=(3-m)!\delta(3-m,3-n)C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{3-n}
and to bound all
other terms we introduce the following result.

Lemma 3.5
.

Under Assumption
1
(in particular if
F\in C^{8}(\mathbbm{R})
with exponentially growing derivatives) we have, for
every
0\leqslant m,n\leqslant 3
and
m\leqslant n
:

|\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{4-n}\varepsilon^{1+\frac{n-m}{2}+i}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{4-m+i}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{3-\frac{m+n}{2}+\delta},\qquad\forall\delta\in[0,1].

Moreover for every
0\leqslant m,n\leqslant 3
,

\begin{array}[]{lllll}|\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\frac{m+n-3}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{m}&\text{if}&m\geqslant 4-n,\\
\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]&=&0&\text{if}&m\char 60\relax 4-n.\end{array}

Proof.

Using formula (
B.4
) we decompose

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\delta^{4-m}(G_{[1]}^{[4-m]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{1}}h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes 4-m})\delta^{4-n}(G_{[1]}^{[4-n]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{2}}h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes 4-n})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{4-n}\binom{4-m}{i}\binom{4-n}{i}i!\mathbbm{E}(G_{[5-n-i]}^{[8-m-n-i]}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[8-n-i]}G_{[5-m-i]}^{[8-m-n-i]}\Phi^{[8-m-i]}_{\zeta_{2}})\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle^{8-m-n-i}.

=\sum_{i=0}^{4-n}\binom{4-m}{i}\binom{4-n}{i}i!\mathbbm{E}(G_{[5-n-i]}^{[8-m-n-i]}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[8-n-i]}G_{[5-m-i]}^{[8-m-n-i]}\Phi^{[8-m-i]}_{\zeta_{2}})\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle^{8-m-n-i}

We can bound the term

\varepsilon^{\frac{m+n}{2}+i-5}\mathbbm{E}(G_{[5-n-i]}^{[8-m-n-i]}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[8-n-i]}G_{[5-m-i]}^{[8-m-n-i]}\Phi^{[8-m-i]}_{\zeta_{2}})\lesssim\left\|\varepsilon^{\frac{n+i-5}{2}}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[8-n-i]}\right\|_{L^{2}}\left\|\varepsilon^{\frac{m+i-5}{2}}\Phi_{\zeta_{2}}^{[8-m-i]}\right\|_{L^{2}}

knowing
8-n-i\vee 8-m-i\leqslant 8
derivatives of

F_{\varepsilon}
(see Remark
3.2
) and using the bound

\varepsilon|C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})|\lesssim 1
of
Lemma
A.12
with
|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|=|C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})|
we have:

|\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{4-n}\varepsilon^{1+\frac{n-m}{2}+i}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{4-m+i}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{3-\frac{m+n}{2}+\delta}.

For the second bound we recall that
\llbracket Y^{m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket=\delta^{m}(h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m})

(Remark
B.12
) and compute

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}\zeta_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\delta^{m}(h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m})\delta^{4-n}(G_{[1]}^{[4-n]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{2}}h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes 4-n})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{m\wedge 4-n}\binom{m}{i}\binom{4-n}{i}i!\mathbbm{E}(\langle\mathrm{D}^{4-n-i}(h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m}),G_{[m+1-i]}^{[m+4-n-i]}\Phi^{[4+m-i]}_{\zeta_{2}}h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes m+4-n-i}\rangle_{H^{\otimes m+4-n-i}}).

Since
\mathrm{D}h^{\otimes m}_{\zeta_{1}}=0
we obtain
\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]=0
if
m\char 60\relax 4-n
and

\displaystyle|\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{m}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\frac{m+n-3}{2}}\mathbbm{E}[\varepsilon^{-\frac{3-m-n}{2}}G_{[m+n-3]}^{[m]}\Phi^{[m+n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{m}

if
m\geqslant 4-n
, with

\mathbbm{E}[\varepsilon^{-\frac{3-m-n}{2}}G_{[m+n-3]}^{[m]}\Phi^{[m+n]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\lesssim 1.

Using Lemma
3.5
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 9\mathbbm{E}[(f_{3,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}})(f_{3,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})]=18(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}[C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})]^{2}+\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]

and thus
G(t-s,x)=18(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s}(x_{1}^{\prime})[C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})]^{2}+\hat{G}(t-s,x)
with the remainder term defined
by

\hat{G}(t-s,x):=\int_{x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s}(x_{1}^{\prime})\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{0}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{(t-s,x_{2})}].

We have the estimation

|\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{2+\delta}.

(3.18)

Similarly

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\varepsilon^{-1/2}f_{2,\varepsilon}\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket(f_{3,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})]=6\varepsilon^{-1/2}f_{2,\varepsilon}f_{3,\varepsilon}[C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})]^{2},

and

\begin{array}[]{lll}|\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|&=&|\mathbbm{E}[(f_{3,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}})(6f_{3,\varepsilon}Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}+\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}})]|\lesssim|f_{3,\varepsilon}||\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|+|\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|\\
&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\delta}(|f_{3,\varepsilon}|+1)C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{2+\delta},\end{array}

(3.19)

and

\begin{array}[]{lll}|\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]|&=&|\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}(3f_{3,\varepsilon}\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon{,}\zeta_{2}}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}^{[1]})]|\lesssim|f_{3,\varepsilon}|\mathbbm{E}[\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]+\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\\
&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{1/2}(|f_{3,\varepsilon}|+1)C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{3}.\end{array}

(3.20)

We have by Lemma
A.16
that for all
\delta\in(0,1)

\begin{array}[]{lll}|\hat{G}(t-s,x)|&\lesssim&\varepsilon^{\delta}(|t-s|^{1/2}+|x|)^{-5-\delta}\end{array}.
Using estimate (
A.19
) together with
Lemma
A.10
, we have that for all
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,\delta)
that
|H(t,x)|\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta^{\prime}}(|t-s|^{1/2}+|x|)^{-\delta}
. Furthermore, we have

\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}=6(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t+s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}

with the remainder term
\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}
given by

\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}=\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]\hat{G}(t-s,x).

The term

6(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t+s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}

can be shown to converge in law to

6(\lambda_{3})^{2}\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y(t+s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y(t,\bar{x})]P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}(Y(0,0)Y(s,x))^{2}

in
C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}
with the standard techniques used
in the analysis of the
\Phi^{4}_{3}
model. On the other hand, for all
\delta>0
sufficiently small we have the bounds

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}+\left\|\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\delta}\|Y_{\varepsilon}\|_{C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}}2^{q(1/2+2\kappa+2\delta)}\int_{s,x}(|x|+|t-s|^{1/2})^{\delta-5}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\delta}\|Y_{\varepsilon}\|_{C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}}2^{q(1/2+2\kappa+2\delta)},

which shows that these remainders go to zero in
\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
, since
\|Y_{\varepsilon}\|_{C^{\kappa}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}}
is bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega)
. Moreover, it is easy
to see that
\|\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}-\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\sim O_{L^{\infty}}(2^{q(1/2+2\kappa+2\delta)})
. Note that

\int_{s,x}G(t-s,x)=\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[1]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(s,x)}]=18(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}+\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{0}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{(s,x)}],

\int_{x}H(t,x)=\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\Phi^{[2]}_{(s,x)}]=\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{(s,x)}].

Here we used the fact that

\int_{x}\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)=\int_{x}\sum_{i,j}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)=\delta(x_{1}^{\prime}),

since
\int_{x}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(0)=0
, where
|i-j|>1
. This
is readily seen in Fourier space taking into account the support properties of
the Littlewood-Paley blocks. Now,

\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{0}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{(s,x)}],\qquad\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{(s,x)}],

converge to finite constants due to the bounds (
3.18
)
and (
3.19
) and by Lemma
A.13

\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)^{2}\lesssim|\log\varepsilon|.

Finally, from (
3.20
) we have

\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{0}\Phi^{[1]}_{(s,x)}]=O(\varepsilon^{-1/2}).

Indeed Lemma
A.13
again yields
\varepsilon\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)^{3}\lesssim 1
.

Thus
\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathbbm{E}[\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}}]\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}
gives a diverging constant
which depends on all the
(f_{n,\varepsilon})_{n}
. Making the choice to define
the renormalisation constants
d^{\tau}
as in eq. (
1.7
) we
cancel exactly these contributions which are either

(F_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
dependent and/or diverging. In particular we
verify that we can satisfy the constraint (
2.3
).

Finally, noting that
\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}=(1-J_{0})
and
\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\mathrm{D}^{2}=(1-J_{0}-J_{1})
(as seen in (
B.3
)),
we can write the trees of (
3.16
) as

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}(1-J_{0})(\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket)\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\\
&&+\frac{f_{3,\varepsilon}}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket+\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\frac{1}{9}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}},\\
\Delta_{q}\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{2,\varepsilon}f_{3,\varepsilon}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}(1-J_{0})(\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket)\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}},\\
\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket Y_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\\
&&+\frac{f_{3,\varepsilon}}{6}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(6\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}+\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\frac{1}{6}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}(\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}},\\
\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}(1-J_{1})(\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket)\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})\\
&&+6(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t+s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}+\\
&&+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\mathrm{D}^{2}(3\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket+\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\mathrm{D}^{2}(\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}.\end{array}

(3.21)

Let us summarize our results so far. We have shown that
\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})+\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})\sim O_{L^{\infty}}(\varepsilon^{\delta}2^{q(1/2+2\kappa+2\delta)})
in
L^{p}(\Omega)
and then these terms converge to
0
in the
right topology as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. As already mentioned, the
convergence in law of

\displaystyle(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}(1-J_{0})(\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket)\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle(\lambda_{3})^{2}\Delta_{q}Y^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}f_{2,\varepsilon}f_{3,\varepsilon}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}(1-J_{0})(\llbracket Y^{2}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket)\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\lambda_{3}\lambda_{2}\Delta_{q}Y^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket Y_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle(\lambda_{3})^{2}\Delta_{q}Y^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

\displaystyle(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}(1-J_{1})(\llbracket Y^{3}_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta_{2}}^{2}\rrbracket)\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle+6(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t+s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle(\lambda_{3})^{2}\Delta_{q}Y^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})

is easy to establish with standard techniques (as done
in
[
4
]
,
[
18
]
)
assuming the convergence of
\lambda_{\varepsilon}
as in (
1.9
)
to
\lambda
. Then, comparing (
3.21
) with the canonical trees
in (
2.10
) we can identify the remainder terms
\Delta_{q}\hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\tau}
that still need to be bounded, that are
precisely those in which
\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta}
appears. Estimating these
terms is the content of next section.

3.4.2
Estimation of renormalised composite trees

In this section we prove the bound (
3.7
) for composite trees.
The difficulty we encounter here is that the remainder

\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}
cannot be written as an iterated Skorohod
integral as in Section
3.1
, but instead as a product of iterated
Skorohod integrals. We will then use the product
formula (
B.6
) to write the remainder in the desired
form. We can write (
3.21
) in a much shorter way as:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{9}\delta G_{1}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}},\\
\Delta_{q}\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{3}\delta G_{1}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}(\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}},\\
\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{6}\delta G_{1}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}},\\
\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})&=&\frac{1}{3}\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}^{2}(\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}(\bar{\zeta})+\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{q}\hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}\\
&&+6(f_{3,\varepsilon})^{2}\int_{s,x}[\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t+s,\bar{x}-x)-\Delta_{q}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,\bar{x})]P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2},\end{array}

just substituting again
\delta G_{1}\mathrm{D}=(1-J_{0})
and
\delta^{2}G_{[1]}^{[2]}\mathrm{D}^{2}=(1-J_{0}-J_{1})
. In order to treat all trees at the
same time, we can write the first terms in the r.h.s. above (modulo a constant
that we discard) as:

\delta^{r}G^{[r]}_{[1]}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}^{r}(\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\quad\text{with $r=1,i+j=2$ or $r=2,i+j=1$.}

First notice that by Lemma
B.2
we have

\left\|\delta^{r}G_{[1]}^{[r]}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}^{r}(\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim\sum_{\theta=0}^{r}\left\|\mathrm{D}^{\theta}G_{[1]}^{[r]}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}^{r}(\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}(H^{\otimes r+\theta})}

and from the boundedness of the operator
\mathrm{D}^{\theta}G_{[1]}^{[r]}
given
by Corollary
B.6
we obtain:

\left\|\delta^{r}G_{[1]}^{[r]}\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}^{r}(\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\lesssim\left\|\int_{\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\mathrm{D}^{r}(\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_{2}})\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,H^{\otimes r})}.

Computing the
r
-th derivative of the integrand we obtain

\Phi^{[4-m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\Phi^{[4-n]}_{\zeta_{2}}h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h^{\otimes\ell}_{\zeta_{2}}=\left[\frac{3!f_{3,\varepsilon}}{(3-m)!}\llbracket Y_{\zeta_{1}}^{m-1}\rrbracket+\widehat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{1}}^{[4-m]}\right]\left[\frac{3!f_{3,\varepsilon}}{(3-n)!}\llbracket Y_{\zeta_{2}}^{n-1}\rrbracket+\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{2}}^{[4-n]}\right]h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes\ell}

(3.22)

for
m+n=5
and
0\leqslant k+\ell\leqslant 2
. The constraints on
m,n,k,\ell
are related to the number of branches in the graphical notation of
the trees: each tree has
m+k-1
leaves with height 2 and
n+\ell-1

leaves with height 1, as follows

\begin{array}[]{lll}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}&\leftrightarrow&m+k=3,\quad n+\ell=3\\
\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}&\leftrightarrow&m=3,k=0,n=2,\ell=1\\
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}&\leftrightarrow&m+k=4,\quad n+\ell=2\\
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}&\leftrightarrow&m+k=4,\quad n+\ell=3.\end{array}

(3.23)

In (
3.22
), the terms which do not contain

\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta}
will generate finite contributions in the limit, as
seen in Section
3.4.1
by writing the
decomposition (
3.21
). We just consider the terms proportional
to
\hat{\Phi}^{[4-m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[4-n]}_{\zeta_{2}}
, because
all the other similar terms featuring at least one remainder

\widehat{\Phi}_{\zeta}^{[m]}
can be estimated with exactly the same
technique, and are easily shown to be vanishing in the appropriate topology.

We can use one of the key observations of this paper, the product
formula (
B.6
), to rewrite products of Skorohod
integrals in the form
\delta^{m}(u)\delta^{n}(v)
as a sum of iterated
Skorohod integrals
\delta^{\ell}(w)
, which are bounded in
L^{p}
by
Lemma
B.2
. We obtain

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}^{[4-m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}^{[4-n]}_{\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{m}(G_{[1]}^{[m]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{1}}h^{\otimes m}_{\zeta_{1}})\delta^{n}(G_{[1]}^{[n]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{2}}h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(q,r,i)\in I}C_{{}_{q,r,i}}\delta^{m+n-q-r}(\langle\mathrm{D}^{r-i}G_{[1]}^{[m]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{1}}h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m},\mathrm{D}^{q-i}G_{[1]}^{[n]}\Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_{2}}h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes n}\rangle_{H^{\otimes q+r-i}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(q,r,i)\in I}C_{{}_{q,r,i}}\varepsilon^{1+\frac{r+q}{2}-i}\delta^{m+n-q-r}(\langle\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m+r-i},\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2})h^{\otimes n+q-i}_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle_{H^{\otimes q+r-i}})

with
I=\{(q,r,i)\in\mathbbm{N}^{3}:0\leqslant q\leqslant m,0\leqslant r\leqslant n,0\leqslant i\leqslant q\wedge r\}
and the
notation shortcut:

\Theta_{[i]}^{[j]}(\zeta):=\varepsilon^{-\frac{i}{2}}G^{[j]}_{[i]}\Phi_{\zeta}^{[3+i]}.

By Remark
B.3
, for every
n,m\geqslant 1
and
\Psi\in\operatorname{Dom}\delta^{n}
we can write
\delta^{n}(\Psi)h^{\otimes m}=\delta^{n}(\Psi\otimes h^{\otimes m})
, and therefore

\int\hat{\Phi}^{[4-m]}_{\zeta_{1}}\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_{2}}^{[4-n]}h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h^{\otimes\ell}_{\zeta_{2}}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=

\sum_{I}C_{{}_{q,r,i}}\varepsilon^{\frac{2+q+r-2i}{2}}\delta^{m+n-q-r}\int\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2})h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m-q}\otimes h^{\otimes n-r}_{\zeta_{2}}\otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h^{\otimes\ell}_{\zeta_{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

With the term to estimate in this form, we can proceed as in
Section
3.1
to estimate separately the terms
\Theta_{[i]}^{[j]}(\zeta)=\varepsilon^{-\frac{i}{2}}G^{[j]}_{[i]}\Phi_{\zeta}^{[3+i]}
in

L^{p}(\Omega)
, which are bounded as discussed in
Remark
3.2
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Under Assumption
1
(in particular if

F_{\varepsilon}\in C^{8}(\mathbbm{R})
and the first 8 derivatives have
exponential growth) we have the bound:

\left\|\delta^{m+n-q-r}\int\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2})h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m-q}\otimes h^{\otimes n-r}_{\zeta_{2}}\otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h^{\otimes\ell}_{\zeta_{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{p}(H^{\otimes k+\ell})}

\lesssim\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|.

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
B.2
the integral can be estimated with

\sum_{j=0,h\leqslant j}^{m+n-q-r}\left\|\int\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2})h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m-q}\otimes h^{\otimes n-r}_{\zeta_{2}}\otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h_{\zeta_{2}}^{\otimes\ell}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{p}(V)},

with
V=H^{\otimes m+k+n+\ell-q-r+j}
. We have that
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(H^{\otimes k+\ell})}^{2}=\|\|\cdot\|^{2}_{H^{\otimes k+\ell}}\|_{L^{p/2}}^{1/2}
and therefore we can bound each term in the
sum above as

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\int\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2}),\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{1})\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{2})\rangle_{H^{\otimes j}}\|_{L^{p/2}}\times

\displaystyle\times|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|)^{1/2}

Using Hölder’s inequality we get the estimate

\displaystyle\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2}),\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{1})\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{2})\rangle_{H^{\otimes j}}\|_{L^{p/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1}),\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{1})\rangle_{H^{\otimes h}}\|_{L^{p}}\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2}),\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{2})\rangle_{H^{\otimes j-h}}\|_{L^{p}}

Now to bound
\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}(\zeta),\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}(\zeta^{\prime})\rangle_{H^{\otimes h}}\|_{L^{p}}
(with
h\leqslant j\leqslant m+n-q-r
and
a=r-i
) we
can use the boundedness of the operator
\mathrm{D}^{h}G_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}
for

h\leqslant 2m
given by Corollary
B.6
. Consider the two
regions
h\leqslant 2m
and
h>2m
. In the first region we just use
Corollary
B.6
to obtain:

\displaystyle\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}(\zeta),\mathrm{D}^{h}\Theta_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}(\zeta^{\prime})\rangle_{H^{\otimes h}}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{D}^{h}G_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi^{[4+a]}_{\zeta_{1}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{4p}(H^{\otimes h})}\left\|\mathrm{D}^{h}G_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi^{[4+a]}_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{4p}(H^{\otimes h})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi^{[4+a]}_{\zeta_{1}}\right\|_{L^{4p}}^{2}\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi^{[4+a]}_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|_{L^{4p}}^{2}.

If
h>2m
we first use the bound
\mathrm{D}^{2m}G_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}
and
then take the remaining
h-2m
derivatives on
\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi^{[4+a]}_{\zeta}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{D}^{h}G_{[1+a]}^{[m+a]}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[4+a]}\right\|_{L^{4p}(H^{\otimes h})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{D}^{h-2m}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a}{2}}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[4+a]}\right\|_{L^{4p}(H^{\otimes h-2m})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\varepsilon^{-\frac{1+a+h-2m}{2}}\Phi_{\zeta_{1}}^{[4+a+h-2m]}\right\|_{L^{4p}(H^{\otimes h-2m})}

From Remark
3.2
we see that this last term is bounded by
a constant if
F\in C^{4+a+h-2m}(\mathbbm{R})
with the first
4+a+h-2m
derivatives having an exponential growth, with
a=r-i
and

h\leqslant m+n-q-r
.

Applying the same reasoning to
\|\langle\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2}),\mathrm{D}^{j-h}\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta^{\prime}_{2})\rangle_{H^{\otimes j-h}}\|_{L^{p}}
we conclude that we
need to control
4+n\vee 4+m
derivatives of
F_{\varepsilon}
in order
to perform the estimates of this Lemma. From the
constraints (
3.23
) we see that
4+n\vee 4+m\leqslant 8
.

From Lemma
3.6
we obtain
\forall\delta\in[0,1/2)
:

\varepsilon^{\frac{2+q+r-2i}{2}}

\left\|\varepsilon^{\frac{2+q+r-2i}{2}}\delta^{m+n-q-r}\int\Theta^{[m+r-i]}_{[1+r-i]}(\zeta_{1})\Theta_{[1+q-i]}^{[n+q-i]}(\zeta_{2})h_{\zeta_{1}}^{\otimes m-q}\otimes h^{\otimes n-r}_{\zeta_{2}}\otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_{1}}\otimes h^{\otimes\ell}_{\zeta_{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\mu_{{}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}}\right\|_{L^{p}(H^{\otimes k+\ell})}

\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta}\left(\varepsilon^{2+q+r-2i-\delta}\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\left|\mu_{{}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}}\right|\left|\mu_{{}_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}}\right|\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

:=\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}(\mathfrak{I})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Our aim now is to estimate the quantity
\mathfrak{I}
. The idea is to use the
bound
\varepsilon|\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle|=\varepsilon C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta-\zeta^{\prime})\lesssim 1
of Lemma
A.12
to
cancel strategically some of the covariances
|\langle h_{\zeta},h_{\zeta^{\prime}}\rangle|
. We will consider three regions:

If
q+r\leqslant 2
we use the bounds

\varepsilon^{q+r-2i}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{r}

and then (we suppose
r\char 60\relax 2
)

\varepsilon^{2-r-\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}\lesssim|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-2+\delta}

to obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{r-\delta}\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-2}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{r}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

(3.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-2+\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|.

(If vice-versa
q\char 60\relax 2
it suffices to put
\delta
on the term
|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+\delta}
.) Notice that in this case
m+k-q>0.

In the case
q+r=3
if
m+k-q\geqslant 2
we estimate like before to
obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2-\delta}\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{\frac{q+r}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{\frac{q+r}{2}}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

(3.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{1+\delta}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|.

Note that
m+k-q+\delta-1>0
and
m+k-q+2\delta-3>-1

here. If
m+k-q=1
we bound

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r-2}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{\frac{3+\delta}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{\frac{3+\delta}{2}}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

(3.26)

and note that
m+k-q-1/2+\delta/2>0
,
m+k-q-1+\delta>0
,
n+\ell-r-2\geqslant 0
. Finally if
m+k-q=0
we can only
have
m+k=3,q=3,r=0,i=0
and thus

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3-2\delta}\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\delta}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

(3.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell+m+k-6}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\delta}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

If
q+r\geqslant 4
we bound first

\varepsilon^{2q+2r-2i+\delta-4}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{q+r-i}\lesssim|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}

(note that
2q+2r-2i+\delta-4\geqslant\delta
) to obtain:

\mathfrak{I}\lesssim\varepsilon^{6-q-r-\delta}\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

Now in the cases
m+k=3,n+\ell=3
and
m+k=4,n+\ell=2
we
can just write
\varepsilon^{6-q-r-\delta}=\varepsilon^{m+k-q}\varepsilon^{6-m-k-r-\delta}
and cancel the corresponding number of
covariances to obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

(3.28)

while for the case
m+k=4,n+\ell=3
we have either
\ell\geqslant 1
or
k\geqslant 1
and therefore with one of the following bounds

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{m+k-1-q}\varepsilon^{n+\ell-r-\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle||\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{\delta}

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{m+k-q}\varepsilon^{n+\ell-1-r-\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{m+k-q}|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n+\ell-r}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{1+\delta}

we obtain the estimates

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{1+\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|

(3.29)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle||\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{\delta}|\langle h_{\zeta_{1}},h_{\zeta_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\langle h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{1}},h_{\zeta^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle|^{2-\frac{\delta}{2}}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|.

(3.30)

We can use directly Lemma
A.17
to obtain a final
estimate of (
3.24
), (
3.25
),
(
3.26
), (
3.29
). For
(\ref{e:graph-est-4}),(\ref{e:graph-est-5})
and (
3.30
) notice that the integral over

\zeta_{1},\zeta^{\prime}_{1}
is finite and thus the whole quantity is proportional to

|\langle h_{\zeta_{2}},h_{\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle|^{n}
. Globally, we have

\mathfrak{I}\lesssim 2^{(m+k+n+\ell-6)q}

as needed to prove (
3.7
).

Remark 3.7
.

Finally, by controlling one more derivative of

F_{\varepsilon}
as done in Section
3.3
, we can
show (
3.8
) for
Y^{\tau}=Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}},\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}
, thus proving that
\hat{Y}^{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in
C^{\kappa/2}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha-\kappa}
in probability

\forall\alpha\char 60\relax|\tau|
. From the proof of Lemma
3.6

together with this observation, we conclude that we need to control the
derivatives of
F_{\varepsilon}
up to order
9
to be able to show the
convergence for composite trees.

4
Convergence of the remainder and a-priori bounds

In this section we prove the convergence of the remainder
(Lemma
4.5
), as well as some technical results on the norm of
the solution, needed in the proof of Theorem
2.2
. In order to
prove Lemma
4.5
we need first to prove
Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.2
and
Lemma
4.3
in this order.

4.1
Boundedness of the remainder

We show that the remainder
R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})
that appears in
equation (
2.6
) can be controlled by a stochastic term

M_{\varepsilon,\delta}
that converges to zero in probability, times a
function of the solution
v_{\varepsilon}
. Let.

M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon}):=\varepsilon^{\delta/2}\|e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}|Y_{\varepsilon}|+c\varepsilon^{1/2}|P_{\cdot}(u_{0,\varepsilon}-Y_{\varepsilon}(0))|}\|_{L^{p}[0,T]L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}.

(4.1)

for
p\in[1,\infty)
,
\delta\in[0,1]
and define

v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}:=v_{\varepsilon}-v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\quad\text{with}\quad v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}:t\mapsto P_{t}(u_{0,\varepsilon}-Y_{\varepsilon}(0)).

(4.2)

Lemma 4.1
(Boundedness of remainder).

For every
\gamma\in(0,1)
,
\delta\in[0,1]
we
have

\|R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon},v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})(t,x)\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma/(3+\delta),p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}\lesssim M_{\varepsilon}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})\|v_{\varepsilon}\|^{3+\delta}_{\mathcal{M}_{T}^{\gamma/(3+\delta)}L^{\infty}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}}

with
M_{\varepsilon}
as in (
4.1
),
v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}
as
in (
4.2
) and
\mathcal{M}^{\gamma/(3+\delta),p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})
,
\mathcal{M}_{T}^{\gamma/(3+\delta)}L^{\infty}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})
defined in (
A.1
).

Proof.

We can write the remainder in two ways:

\displaystyle R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{\varepsilon}^{3}\int_{0}^{1}[F_{\varepsilon}^{(3)}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}+\tau\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{\varepsilon})-F_{\varepsilon}^{(3)}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon})]\frac{(1-\tau)^{2}}{2!}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\tfrac{1}{2}}v_{\varepsilon}^{4}\int_{0}^{1}F_{\varepsilon}^{(4)}(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\varepsilon}+\tau\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}v_{\varepsilon})\frac{(1-\tau)^{3}}{3!}\mathrm{d}\tau.

From assumption (
1.8
) on
F_{\varepsilon}
we obtain by
interpolation of these two expressions,
\forall\delta\in[0,1]
,

\forall t\geqslant 0,x\in\mathbbm{T}^{3}
,

|R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})(t,x)|\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta/2}|v_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|^{3+\delta}e^{c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|+c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|+c|\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|},

and we estimate,
\forall\gamma\in[0,1)
,

\|t\mapsto t^{\gamma}R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})(t,x)\|_{L^{p}((0,T],L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3}))}\lesssim\|t^{\frac{\gamma}{3+\delta}}v_{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}^{3+\delta}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}}\left\|\varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}}e^{c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|+c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|}\right\|_{L^{p}[0,T]L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}.

We can also verify that
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightarrow 0
in probability
for every
\delta>0
:

Lemma 4.2
(Convergence of the stochastic term).

Under Assumption
1
the random variable

M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})
defined
in (
4.1
) converges to zero in probability
\forall\delta\in(0,1]
.

Proof.

We can use Young’s inequality to estimate
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})
for some
c^{\prime}>0
as

\displaystyle M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\delta/2}\|e^{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{1/2}|Y_{\varepsilon}|}\|_{L^{p}[0,T]L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}+\varepsilon^{\delta/2}\|e^{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{1/2}|P_{.}Y_{\varepsilon}(0)|}\|_{L^{p}[0,T]L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{\delta/2}T^{1/p}e^{c^{\prime}\|\varepsilon^{1/2}u_{0,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}}.

Under Assumptions
1
the term
\|\varepsilon^{1/2}u_{0,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}
is uniformly bounded, so the
third term above converges to zero in probability. Note that
\varepsilon^{1/2}Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)
and
P_{t}\varepsilon^{1/2}Y_{\varepsilon}(t=0)
are centered Gaussian random variables, and then both
\mathbbm{E}\|e^{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{1/2}|Y_{\varepsilon}|}\|^{p}_{L^{p}[0,T]L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}
and
\mathbbm{E}\|e^{c^{\prime}\varepsilon^{1/2}|P_{.}Y_{\varepsilon}(0)|}\|^{p}_{L^{p}[0,T]L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}
are uniformly
bounded in
\varepsilon>0
for every
p\in[1,\infty)
. This yields the
convergence in probability of
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})
.

In order to show that
\|R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma^{\prime},p}_{T}L^{p}}\rightarrow 0
in probability for
\gamma^{\prime}>\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3}{2}\kappa
as needed in the proof of
Theorem
2.2
, we still need to control the norms
\|v_{\varepsilon}\|^{3+\delta}_{\mathcal{M}_{T}^{\gamma/(3+\delta)}L^{\infty}}
and
\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}
that appear in
Lemma
4.1
. This is done in next section.

4.2
Apriori bounds on the solution

Lemma 4.3
(Apriori bound on the solution).

Fix
T>0
. There exists
\kappa>0
,
T_{\star}=T_{\star}\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}},\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}},|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|\right)\in(0,T]
a
lower semicontinuous function depending only on
\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}},\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}},|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|\right)
and a
collection of events
(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
such
that

\mathbbm{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 1\qquad\text{as
$\varepsilon\rightarrow 0$}

and conditionally on
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}
there exists a universal
constant
C>0
such that:

\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{T_{\star}}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-\frac{1}{2}-\kappa}}+\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{M}_{T_{\star}}^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3\kappa}{2}}L^{\infty}}\leqslant C(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\left(1+\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)^{3}

for any
v_{\varepsilon}
that solves equation (
2.6
).
Moreover, still conditionally on
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}
we have

\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{C_{T_{\star}}L^{\infty}}\leqslant C(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\left(1+\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)^{3}

with
v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}
as in (
4.2
).

Proof.

We know from Lemma
4.4
that the bounds above on
\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{T_{\star}}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-\frac{1}{2}-\kappa}}+\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{M}_{T_{\star}}^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3\kappa}{2}}L^{\infty}}
and
\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{C_{T_{\star}}L^{\infty}}
hold whenever
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}\leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa/2}
. The event
\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}=\{M_{\varepsilon,\delta}\leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa/2}\}
has

\mathbbm{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon})\rightarrow 1
by
Lemma
4.2
and this proves the result.

The only thing left to prove is Lemma
4.4
, which just a
standard application of some well-known bounds on paraproducts, that are
recalled in Appendices
A.2
, and
A.2
.

First observe that for
\varepsilon>0
a pair
(v_{\varepsilon},v^{\natural}_{\varepsilon})
solves the paracontrolled
equation (
2.6
) if and only if
v^{\natural}_{\varepsilon}=v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
and
(v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon})
solves:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}v_{\varepsilon}&=&-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}-\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}-3v_{\varepsilon}\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}+v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\\
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997ptv_{\varepsilon}^{\flat}&=&U(\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon};v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})-R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\\
v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}(0)&=&Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}(0)+\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}(0)+3v_{\varepsilon,0}\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}(0)\end{array}\right.

(4.3)

Here
U
is the same as in (
2.6
). The initial condition of
(
4.3
) is given by
v_{\varepsilon,0}:=u_{0,\varepsilon}-Y_{\varepsilon}(0)
. The a-priori bounds of
Lemma
4.4
come from being able to find closed estimates
for (
4.3
).

Let us specify now all the notations we are going to use in the rest of this
section. We consider the spaces

\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}:=\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{2\kappa}_{T}\cap\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{1/4,1/2+2\kappa}_{T}\cap\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{1/2,1+2\kappa}_{T},\quad\mathcal{V}_{T}:=\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{1/2,1/2-\kappa}_{T}\cap\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt_{T}^{1/4+3\kappa/2,2\kappa},

with the corresponding norms

\displaystyle\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{2\kappa}_{T}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{1/4,1/2+2\kappa}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\flat}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{1/2,1+2\kappa}_{T}},

(4.4)

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{1/2,1/2-\kappa}}+\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{1/4+3\kappa/2,2\kappa}}.

(4.5)

We refer to Appendix
A.1
for the definition of the parabolic
spaces
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}
. We let

\begin{array}[]{lllll}v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}&:=&v_{\varepsilon}-v^{\sharp}&=&-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}-\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}-3(v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}+v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon},\\
v_{\varepsilon}^{\boxslash}&:=&v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}&=&-\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}-3(v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}+v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon},\end{array}

and
v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}(t=0)=v_{\varepsilon}^{\boxslash}(t=0)=0
. We define also the norm

\displaystyle\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\|v^{\Box}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{2\kappa}_{T}}+\|v^{\Box}\|_{\mathcal{M}^{1/4}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{1/2+2\kappa}},

with
\mathcal{M}^{1/4}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{1/2+2\kappa}
given in
Appendix
A.1
. In order not to get lost in these definitions the
reader can keep in mind the following:

-

v_{\varepsilon}
is the solution without the linear term;

-

v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}
is the contribution of the initial
condition, which give origin to some explosive norm (near the initial time);

-

v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}
is the regular part of the solution;

-

v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon},v_{\varepsilon}^{\boxslash}
enter in the
estimation of the remainder, they are just convenient shortcuts for certain
contributions appearing in
v_{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists
T_{\star}=T_{\star}\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}},\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}},|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|\right)\in(0,T]
a
lower semicontinuous function depending only on
\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}
,
\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}
and
|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|
, a constant

M_{\varepsilon,\delta}=M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})>0
defined by (
4.1
), and a universal constant
C>0
such that, whenever
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}\leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa/2}
we have

\displaystyle\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T_{\star}}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\left(1+\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)^{3},

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T_{\star}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T_{\star}}^{\flat}}\right).

Proof.

Using the well-known Schauder estimates of Lemma
A.2
(and
the fact that
\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{\kappa,\alpha}}\lesssim T^{\kappa}\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{\alpha}}
) we obtain for
\kappa,\theta>0
small enough
