\|If\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{-\theta+2\kappa,2\kappa}_{T}}+\|If\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{1/4-\theta+2\kappa,1/2+2\kappa}_{T}}+\|If\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{1/2-\theta+2\kappa,1+2\kappa}_{T}}\lesssim T^{\frac{\kappa}{2}}\left(\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{1-\theta}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-\kappa}}+\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{1/2+2\kappa}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}}\right).

(4.6)

We choose
\theta>2\kappa
small enough so that

\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{-\theta+3\kappa/2,2\kappa}_{T}\cap\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{1/4-\theta+3\kappa/2,1/2+2\kappa}_{T}\cap\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{1/2-\theta+3\kappa/2,1+2\kappa}_{T}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}.

Now

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}+\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}+\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{{}_{T}}L^{\infty}}(\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{1-\kappa}}+\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1-\kappa}})

\displaystyle+\left(\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{M}_{T}^{1/4}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-\kappa}}\right)(\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{1-\kappa}}+\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1-\kappa}})+\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\flat}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+T^{\kappa}\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}

where we used that
v^{\Box}(0)=0
and as a consequence that

\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{{}_{T}}L^{\infty}}\leqslant T^{\kappa}\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{C_{{}_{T}}^{\kappa}L^{\infty}}\leqslant T^{\kappa}\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}
to gain a
small power of
T
. So provided
T
is small enough (depending only on

\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}
) this yields the following a-priori estimation
on
v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}
:

\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}\lesssim\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}.

Therefore we have an estimation on
v_{\varepsilon}
:

\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}\leqslant\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}+\|v^{\Box}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}\lesssim\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}\lesssim\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}.

In order to estimate terms in
U(\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon};v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})
we decompose the renormalised products as

\begin{array}[]{lll}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}\hat{\diamond}v_{\varepsilon}&=&v_{\varepsilon}\succ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}-\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}-3v_{\varepsilon}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}+v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}-3\overline{\operatorname{com}}_{1}(v_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}})\\
v_{\varepsilon}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}&=&-\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}Y_{\varepsilon}-3(v_{\varepsilon}\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}})Y_{\varepsilon}+Y_{\varepsilon}\preccurlyeq(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})+Y_{\varepsilon}\succ(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\\
&&-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}\prec Y_{\varepsilon}-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}\succ Y_{\varepsilon}-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}\\
Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond v_{\varepsilon}^{2}&=&Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})^{2}+2(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})(\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}+3v_{\varepsilon}\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}})-2(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})\preccurlyeq(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\\
&&+2(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})\succ(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\preccurlyeq(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp})^{2}+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\succ(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp})^{2}.\end{array}

We decompose
U(\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon};v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})
as

\displaystyle U(\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon};v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q_{-1/2}(\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon},v_{0,\varepsilon},v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon})+Q_{0}(\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon},v_{0,\varepsilon},v_{\varepsilon},v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon})+Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle Q_{-1/2}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-3[v_{\varepsilon}\succ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}-3\overline{\operatorname{com}}_{1}(v_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}})+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\succ(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp})^{2}]

\displaystyle-6[(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})(3v_{\varepsilon}\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}})+(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})\succ(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})]

\displaystyle+2\lambda_{2,\varepsilon}(3(v_{\varepsilon}\prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}})Y_{\varepsilon}-Y_{\varepsilon}\succ(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}))+3\operatorname{com}_{3}(v_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}})+3\operatorname{com}_{2}(v_{\varepsilon},Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}})

\displaystyle Q_{0}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle 3[3v_{\varepsilon}Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}-v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}-v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/2_tree.eps}}}+2(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})\preccurlyeq(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\preccurlyeq(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp})^{2}]

\displaystyle-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing}v_{\varepsilon}^{3}-\lambda_{2,\varepsilon}[v_{\varepsilon}^{2}+2Y_{\varepsilon}\preccurlyeq(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}+v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})]

\displaystyle Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(1-\lambda_{1,\varepsilon})Y_{\varepsilon}-\lambda_{0,\varepsilon}+3[\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/23_tree_res.eps}}}+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/33_tree_res.eps}}}-Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})^{2}-2(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{261234}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/1_tree_mod.eps}}}\diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}})\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}]

\displaystyle+2\lambda_{2,\varepsilon}(\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/21_tree_mod.eps}}}Y_{\varepsilon}+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}\prec Y_{\varepsilon}+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{522469}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/31_tree.eps}}}\succ Y_{\varepsilon}+Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{783703}{}{\includegraphics[]{trees/32_tree_res.eps}}}).

Here
Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}}
does not depend
from the solution but only on
\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}
(as the notation suggests) and we have grouped
the other terms which we expect to have regularity
\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}
in
Q_{-1/2}
, (and the same for
Q_{0}
and regularity
\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-k}
). With the same technique we used above for
v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}
, we
obtain the following estimate on
v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}

\|v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{1/2+3\kappa/2,1/2+2\kappa}}+\|v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{1/4+\kappa,\kappa}}\lesssim\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}

and this yields

\|(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon})^{2}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{3/4+5\kappa/2,1/2+2\kappa}}+\|(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon})^{2}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{1/2+2\kappa,\kappa}}\lesssim\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}\right)^{2}.

Then we are ready to bound
Q_{-1/2},Q_{0},Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}}
using the standard paraproducts estimations
recalled in Appendix
A.2
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\|Q_{-1/2}\|_{\mathcal{M}_{T}^{1/2+2\kappa}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-2\kappa}}+\|Q_{0}\|_{\mathcal{M}^{1-\theta}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-\kappa}}&\lesssim&(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\left(1+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}+\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}\right)^{3}\\
\|Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon},\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}}\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}&\lesssim&(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}.\end{array}

In order to conclude the estimation of
\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}
we have to control
\|IR_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}
. This is achieved easily by
the using the results of Section
4.1
. Thanks to
Lemma
4.1

\forall\delta\in(0,1)
,
\forall\theta>0

such that
\frac{1-\theta}{3+\delta}>\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3\kappa}{2}

(note that it is possible to choose
\theta>2\kappa
that satisfies this
property as long as
k
and
\delta
are small enough) we have:

\|R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{\mathcal{M}^{1-\theta,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}\lesssim M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})\|v_{\varepsilon}\|^{3+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\Box}_{T}}}.

By Lemma
A.3
together with (
A.2
) we
obtain then

\displaystyle\|IR_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})\|v_{\varepsilon}\|^{3+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\Box}}}.

Using that

\|P_{\cdot}v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}(0)\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}\lesssim\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}(0)\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{1/2-2\kappa}}\lesssim\left(1+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}

we obtain that
\exists C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C^{\prime}(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\left(1+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)^{3}+C^{\prime}T^{\kappa/2}(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}^{3}

\displaystyle+C^{\prime}M_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.73616pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}}\|v_{\varepsilon}\|^{3+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle D+CM_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}}+CT^{\kappa/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}^{3}+CM_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{T}^{\flat}}}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{T}}^{3+\delta}

with

C:=C^{\prime}[(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}+e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.73616pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)}\left(1+\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)^{3+\delta}\right)],

and

D:=C^{\prime}(1+|\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}|)(1+\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}})^{3}\left(1+\|v_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}}\right)^{3}.

Let
T_{\star}\in(0,T]
such that:

CT_{\star}^{\kappa/2}[(5C)^{2}+e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}(5C)}(5C)^{2+\delta}]\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\text{, \quad and }CT_{\star}^{\kappa/2}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}(5C)}\leqslant D.

Assume that
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}\leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa/2}
.
Define a closed interval
[0,S]=\{t\in[0,T_{\star}]:\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{t}^{\flat}}\leqslant 4D\}\subseteq[0,T_{\star}]
. This interval is well defined and non–empty
since
t\mapsto\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{t}^{\flat}}
is
continuous and nondecreasing and
\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{0}^{\flat}}\leqslant 4D
. Let us assume that
S\char 60\relax T_{\star}
, then we can take
\epsilon>0
small enough such that
S+\epsilon\char 60\relax T_{\star}
and by continuity
\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{S+\epsilon}^{\flat}}\leqslant 5C
, then

\displaystyle\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{S+\epsilon}^{\flat}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle D+CM_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{S+\epsilon}^{\flat}}}+C(S+\epsilon)^{\kappa/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S+\epsilon}}^{3}+CM_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{S+\epsilon}^{\flat}}}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S+\epsilon}}^{3+\delta}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle D+CM_{\varepsilon,\delta}(Y_{\varepsilon},u_{0,\varepsilon})e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}(5C)}+CT_{\star}^{\kappa/2}(5C)^{2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S+\epsilon}}+CT_{\star}^{\kappa/2}e^{c\varepsilon^{1/2}(5C)}(5C)^{2+\delta}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S+\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 2D+\frac{1}{2}\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S+\epsilon}}

which gives
\|v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon_{n}}\|_{\mathcal{V}_{S+\epsilon}^{\flat}}\leqslant 4D
. This implies
S=T_{\star}
(by
contradiction). From the construction of
T_{\star}
it is easy to see that

T_{\star}\left(\|\mathbbm{Y}_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{X}_{T}},\|u_{\varepsilon,0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-1/2-\kappa}},|\lambda_{\varepsilon}|\right)
is lower semicontinuous.

4.3
Convergence of the remainder

It suffices to put together the results obtained in
Sections
4.1
and
4.2
to obtain the
convergence of
R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})
:

Lemma 4.5
.

The remainder
R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})
that
appears in equation (
2.6
) converges in probability to
0

as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
in the space
\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T_{\star}}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})
.

Proof.

From the estimation on
R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})
of
Lemma
4.1
, together with the fact that
M_{\varepsilon,\delta}\rightarrow 0
in probability (Lemma
4.2
) and the
bounds on
\|v_{\varepsilon}\|_{\mathcal{M}_{T_{\star}}^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3\kappa}{2}}L^{\infty}}
and
\|v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\|_{C_{T}L^{\infty}}
of Lemma
4.3
we see immediately that

\|R_{\varepsilon}(v_{\varepsilon})\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T_{\star}}L^{p}}\rightarrow 0\quad\mbox{ in probability.}

Appendix A
Paracontrolled analysis and kernel
estimations

In this section we first recall the the basic results of paracontrolled
calculus first introduced in
[
6
]
, without
proofs. For more details on Besov spaces, Littlewood–Paley theory, and Bony’s
paraproduct the reader can refer to the monograph
[
2
]
. We
then proceed to give some results on the convolution of functions with known
singularity and the estimation of finite-chaos Gaussian trees. We refer to
Section 10 of
[
8
]
and to the nice pedagogic
exposition
[
18
]
for further details.

A.1
Notation

Throughout the paper, we use the notation
a\lesssim b
if there exists a
constant
c>0
, independent of the variables under consideration, such that

a\leqslant c\cdot b
. If we want to emphasize the dependence of
c
on the
variable
x
, then we write
a(x)\lesssim_{x}b(x)
. If
f
is a map from
A\subset\mathbbm{R}
to the linear space
Y
, then we write
f_{s,t}=f(t)-f(s)
. For
f\in L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{d})
we write
\|f(x)\|^{p}_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}:=\int_{\mathbbm{T}^{3}}|f(x)|^{p}\mathrm{d}x
.

Given a Banach space
X
with norm
\|\cdot\|_{X}
and
T>0
, we note
C_{T}X=C([0,T],X)
for the space of continuous maps from
[0,T]
to
X
,
equipped with the supremum norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{C_{T}X}
, and we set
CX=C(\mathbbm{R}_{+},X)
. For
\alpha\in(0,1)
we also define

C^{\alpha}_{T}X
as the space of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous functions from

[0,T]
to
X
, endowed with the seminorm
\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}_{T}X}=\sup_{0\leqslant s\char 60\relax t\leqslant T}\|f(t)-f(s)\|_{X}/|t-s|^{\alpha}
, and we
write
C^{\alpha}_{\operatorname{loc}}X
for the space of locally
\alpha
-Hölder
continuous functions from
\mathbbm{R}_{+}
to
X
. For
\gamma>0
,
p\in[1,\infty)
, we define

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}X&=&\{v:L^{p}((0,T],X):\|v\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}X}=\|t\mapsto t^{\gamma}v(t)\|_{L^{p}((0,T],X)}\char 60\relax\infty\},\\
\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{T}X&=&\{v:C((0,T],X):\|v\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{T}X}=\|t\mapsto t^{\gamma}v(t)\|_{C_{T}X}\char 60\relax\infty\}.\end{array}

(A.1)

The space of distributions on the torus is denoted by
\mathscr{D}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\prime}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})

or
\mathscr{D}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\prime}
. The Fourier transform is defined with the normalization

\mathscr{F}\hskip 1.00006ptu(k)=\hat{u}(k)=\int_{\mathbbm{T}^{d}}e^{-\iota\langle k,x\rangle}u(x)\mathrm{d}x,\qquad k\in\mathbbm{Z}^{3},

so that the inverse Fourier transform is given by
\mathscr{F}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-1}v(x)=(2\pi)^{-1}\sum_{k}e^{\iota\langle k,x\rangle}v(k)
. Let
(\chi,\rho)

denote a dyadic partition of unity such that
\operatorname{supp}(\rho(2^{-i}\cdot))\cap\operatorname{supp}(\rho(2^{-j}\cdot))=\emptyset
for
|i-j|>1
. The family of operators
(\Delta_{j})_{j\geq-1}
will denote the
Littlewood-Paley projections associated to this partition of unity, that is

\Delta_{-1}u=\mathscr{F}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-1}\left(\chi\mathscr{F}\hskip 1.00006ptu\right)
and
\Delta_{j}=\mathscr{F}\hskip 1.00006pt^{-1}\left(\rho(2^{-j}\cdot)\mathscr{F}\hskip 1.00006ptu\right)
for
j\geq 0
. Let

S_{j}=\sum_{i\char 60\relax j}\Delta_{i}
, and
K_{q}
be the kernel of
\Delta_{q}
so
that

\Delta_{q}f(\bar{x})=\int_{\mathbbm{T}^{3}}K_{\bar{x},q}(x)f(x)\mathrm{d}x.

For the precise definition and properties of the Littlewood-Paley
decomposition
f=\sum_{q\geqslant-1}\Delta_{q}f
in
\mathscr{D}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\prime}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})
, see Chapter 2 of
[
2
]
. The Hölder-Besov
space
B^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbbm{T}^{3},\mathbbm{R})
for
\alpha\in\mathbbm{R}
,
p,q\in[1,\infty]
with
B^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbbm{T}^{3},\mathbbm{R})=:\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{a}
is and equipped with the norm

\begin{array}[]{lll}\lVert f\rVert_{\alpha}=\lVert f\rVert_{B^{\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}}&=&\sup_{i\geqslant-1}(2^{i\alpha}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}),\\
\lVert f\rVert_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q}}&=&\|2^{i\alpha}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}\|_{\ell^{q}}.\end{array}

If
f
is in
\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha-\varepsilon}
for all
\varepsilon>0
, then we
write
f\in\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha-}
. For
\alpha\in(0,2)
, we define the space

\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt_{T}^{\alpha}=C^{\alpha/2}_{T}L^{\infty}\cap C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}
, equipped
with the norm

\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt_{T}^{\alpha}}=\max\left\{\|f\|_{C^{\alpha/2}_{T}L^{\infty}},\|f\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\right\}.

The notation is chosen to be reminiscent of

\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt:=\partial_{t}-,

by which we will always denote the heat operator with periodic boundary
conditions on
\mathbbm{T}^{d}
. We also write
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\alpha}=C^{\alpha/2}_{\operatorname{loc}}L^{\infty}\cap C\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}
. When working with irregular
initial conditions, we will need to consider explosive spaces of parabolic
type. For
\gamma\geqslant 0
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and
T>0
we define the
norm

\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}}=\max\left\{\|t\mapsto t^{\gamma}f(t)\|_{C^{\alpha/2}_{T}L^{\infty}},\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\right\},

and the space
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}=\left\{f:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}:\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}}\char 60\relax\infty\right\}
. In
particular, we have
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{0,\alpha}_{T}=\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\alpha}_{T}
. We introduce the
linear operator
I:C\left(\mathbbm{R}_{+},\mathscr{D}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\prime}(\mathbbm{T})\right)\rightarrow C\left(\mathbbm{R}_{+},\mathscr{D}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\prime}(\mathbbm{T})\right)
given by

If(t)=\int_{0}^{t}P_{t-s}f(s)\mathrm{d}s,

where
(P_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
is the heat semigroup with kernel
P_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}}\mathbbm{I}_{t\geqslant 0}
.

Paraproducts are bilinear operations introduced by Bony
[
3
]
in
order to linearize a class of non-linear PDE problems. They appear naturally
in the analysis of the product of two Besov distributions. In terms of
Littlewood–Paley blocks, the product
fg
of two distributions
f
and
g

can be formally decomposed as

fg=f\prec g+f\succ g+f\circ g,

where

f\prec g=g\succ f:=\sum_{j\geqslant-1}\sum_{i=-1}^{j-2}\Delta_{i}f\Delta_{j}g\quad\text{and}\quad f\circ g:=\sum_{|i-j|\leqslant 1}\Delta_{i}f\Delta_{j}g.

This decomposition behaves nicely with respect to Littlewood–Paley theory. We
call
f\prec g
and
f\succ g

paraproducts
, and
f\circ g
the

resonant
term. We use the notation
f\preccurlyeq g=f\prec g+f\circ g
. The basic result about these bilinear operations is given by the
following estimates, essentially due to Bony
[
3
]
and
Meyer
[
14
]
.

When dealing with paraproducts in the context of parabolic equations it would
be natural to introduce parabolic Besov spaces and related paraproducts. But
to keep a simpler setting, we choose to work with space–time distributions
belonging to the scale of spaces
\left(C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha\in\mathbbm{R}}
for some
T>0
. To do so efficiently, we will use a modified
paraproduct which introduces some smoothing in the time variable that is tuned
to the parabolic scaling. Let therefore
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R},\mathbbm{R}_{+})
be nonnegative with compact support contained in

\mathbbm{R}_{+}
and with total mass
1
, and define for all
i\geqslant-1

the operator

Q_{i}:C\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta}\rightarrow C\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta},\qquad Q_{i}f(t)=\int_{0}^{\infty}2^{-2i}\varphi(2^{2i}(t-s))f(s)\mathrm{d}s.

We will often apply
Q_{i}
and other operators on
C\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta}
to functions

f\in C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta}
which we then simply extend from
[0,T]
to

\mathbbm{R}_{+}
by considering
f(\cdot\wedge T)
. With the help of
Q_{i}
,
we define a modified paraproduct

f\prec\!\!\!\prec g:=\sum_{i}(Q_{i}S_{i-1}f)\Delta_{i}g

for
f,g\in C\left(\mathbbm{R}_{+},\mathscr{D}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\prime}(\mathbbm{T})\right)
. We define
the commutators
\operatorname{com}_{1},\overline{\operatorname{com}}_{1},\operatorname{com}_{2},\operatorname{com}_{3}
in Lemma
A.7
. We write
\check{P}(t,x)=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}}e^{-t}\mathbbm{1}_{t\geqslant 0}
for a modified heat kernel which has the same
bounds of the usual heat kernel
P(t,x)
. Let
Y_{\varepsilon}
as
in (
1.3
) and recall that
C_{\varepsilon}
is the covariance of

Y_{\varepsilon}
, i.e.
C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\mathbbm{E}(Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)Y_{\varepsilon}(0,0))
. We will sometimes write
Y_{\varepsilon,\zeta}:=Y_{\varepsilon}(t,x)
for
\zeta=(t,x)\in\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3}
. Let
\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}=\varepsilon\mathbbm{E}[(Y_{\varepsilon}(0,0))^{2}]=\varepsilon C_{\varepsilon}(0,0)
.

A.2
Basic paracontrolled calculus
results

First, let us recall some interpolation results on the parabolic time-weighted
spaces
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}
:

Lemma A.1
.

For all
\alpha\in(0,2)
,
\gamma\in[0,1)
,

\varepsilon\in[0,\alpha\wedge 2\gamma)
,
T>0
and
f\in\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}
with
f(0)=0
we have

\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma-\varepsilon/2,\alpha-\varepsilon}_{T}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}}.

(A.2)

Let
\alpha\in(0,2)
,
\gamma\in(0,1),

T>0
, and let
f\in\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt^{\alpha}_{T}
. Then for all
\delta\in(0,\alpha]
we have

\begin{array}[]{lll}\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\delta}_{T}}&\lesssim&\|f(0)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\delta}}+T^{(\alpha-\delta)/2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\alpha}_{T}},\\
\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\delta}_{T}}&\lesssim&T^{(\alpha-\delta)/2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}}.\end{array}

(A.3)

Schauder estimates

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,2)
and
\gamma\in[0,1)
. Then

\|If\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{t}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha-2}},

(A.4)

for all
t>0
. If further
\beta\geqslant-\alpha
, then

\|s\mapsto P_{s}u_{0}\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{(\beta+\alpha)/2,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{-\beta}}.

(A.5)

For all
\alpha\in\mathbbm{R}
,
\gamma\in[0,1)
, and
t>0
we have

\|If\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{t}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{t}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha-2}}.

(A.6)

Proofs can be found e.g. in
[
7
]
. We need also some
well known bounds for the solutions of the heat equation with sources in
space–time Lebesgue spaces.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbbm{R}
and
f\in L^{p}_{T}B^{\beta}_{p,\infty}
, then for every
\kappa\in[0,1]
we have
If\in C_{T}^{\kappa/q}\mathcal{\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt}^{\beta+2(1-\kappa)-(2-2\kappa+d)/p}
with

\|If\|_{C_{T}^{\kappa/q}\mathcal{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.70004pt}^{\beta+2(1-\kappa)-(2-2\kappa+d)/p}}\lesssim_{T}\|f\|_{L^{p}_{T}B^{\beta}_{p,\infty}},

with
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{p}=1
. Moreover, for every
\gamma\char 60\relax\gamma^{\prime}\char 60\relax 1-1/p
and every
0\char 60\relax\alpha\char 60\relax(2-5/p+\beta)\wedge 2
we have

\|If\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma^{\prime},\alpha}_{T}}\lesssim_{T}\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}B^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

Proof.

We only show the second inequality as the first one is easier and obtained
with similar techniques. Let
u=If
, we have

\displaystyle t^{\gamma}\|\Delta_{i}u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle t^{1/q}2^{di/p}\left[\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\gamma q}e^{-cq2^{2i}t(1-s)}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{1/q}\left[\int_{0}^{t}s^{\gamma p}\|\Delta_{i}f(s)\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,q}

\displaystyle 2^{id/p}2^{-2i/q}\left[\int_{0}^{t}s^{\gamma p}\|\Delta_{i}f(s)\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{1/p}

which allows us to bound
\|If\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}
.
In order to estimate
\|t\mapsto t^{\gamma^{\prime}}If\|_{C^{\alpha/2}_{T}L^{\infty}}
we write

\displaystyle\|t^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}u(t)-s^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}u(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}v^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\|\Delta_{i}u(v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathrm{d}v+|t-s|2^{i(d+2)/p}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{s}^{t}v^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}f(v)\mathrm{d}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

We can estimate the first term as

\int_{s}^{t}v^{\gamma^{\prime}-1}\|\Delta_{i}u(v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathrm{d}v\lesssim 2^{i(d+2)/p}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}\int_{s}^{t}v^{\gamma^{\prime}-\gamma-1}\mathrm{d}v.

For the third term we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{s}^{t}v^{\gamma}\Delta_{i}f(v)\mathrm{d}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}v\right]^{1/q}\left[\int_{s}^{t}v^{\gamma p}\|\Delta_{i}f(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{p}\mathrm{d}v\right]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{id/p}|t-s|^{1/q}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}

We obtain then if
2^{2i}|t-s|\leqslant 1

\|t^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}u(t)-s^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}u(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{id/p}|t-s|^{1/q}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}

and if
2^{2i}|t-s|>1
we just use the trivial estimate

\|t^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}u(t)-s^{\gamma^{\prime}}\Delta_{i}u(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{id/p}2^{-2i/q}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}\lesssim 2^{id/p}|t-s|^{1/q}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}.

Therefore, for every
\kappa\in[0,1]
:

\|t^{\gamma\prime}\Delta_{i}u(t)-s^{\gamma\prime}\Delta_{i}u(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{(\frac{d+2}{p}-2)i}2^{2\kappa i/q}|t-s|^{\kappa/q}\|\Delta_{i}f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma,p}_{T}L^{p}(\mathbbm{T}^{3})}.

Choosing
\kappa/q=\alpha/2
we obtain the desired estimate.

Estimates on Bony’s paraproducts and commutators

Lemma A.4
.

For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\|f\prec g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}}\lesssim_{\beta}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}},

(A.7)

and for
\alpha\char 60\relax 0
furthermore

\|f\prec g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}}.

(A.8)

For
\alpha+\beta>0
we have

\|f\circ g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}}.

(A.9)

A natural corollary is that the product
fg
of two elements
f\in\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}
and
g\in\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta}
is well defined as soon as
\alpha+\beta>0
, and that it belongs to
\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\gamma}
, where
\gamma=\min\{\alpha,\beta,\alpha+\beta\}
. We will also need a commutator estimation:

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\alpha>0
,
\beta\in\mathbbm{R}
, and
let
f,g\in\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}
, and
h\in\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta}
. Then

\|f\prec(g\prec h)-(fg)\prec h\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\|h\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}}.

We collect in the following lemma various estimates for the modified
paraproduct
f\prec\!\!\!\prec g
, proofs are again in
[
7
]
.

Lemma A.6
.

a)

For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\gamma\in[0,1)
we have

t^{\gamma}\|f\prec\!\!\!\prec g(t)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{t}L^{\infty}}\|g(t)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}},

(A.10)

for all
t>0
, and for
\alpha\char 60\relax 0
furthermore

t^{\gamma}\|f\prec\!\!\!\prec g(t)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_{t}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}\|g(t)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}}.

(A.11)

b)

Let
\alpha,\delta\in(0,2)
,
\gamma\in[0,1)
,
T>0
, and
let
f\in\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt_{T}^{\gamma,\delta}
,
g\in C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}
, and
\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997ptg\in C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha-2}
. Then

\|f\prec\!\!\!\prec g\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{T}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\delta}_{T}}\left(\|g\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha}}+\left\|\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997ptg\right\|_{C_{T}\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\alpha-2}}\right).

(A.12)

We introduce various commutators which allow to control non-linear functions
of paraproducts and also the interaction of the paraproducts with the heat
kernel.

Lemma A.7
.

a)

For
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\mathbbm{R}
such that
\alpha+\beta+\gamma>0
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
there exists bounded trilinear
maps

\operatorname{com}_{1},\overline{\operatorname{com}}_{1}:\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha}\times\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\beta}\times\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\gamma}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}\hskip 1.00006pt^{\alpha+\beta+\gamma},

such that for smooth
f,g,h
they satisfy

\operatorname{com}_{1}(f,g,h)=(f\prec g)\circ h-f(g\circ h).

(A.13)

\overline{\operatorname{com}}_{1}(f,g,h)=(f\prec\!\!\!\prec g)\circ h-f(g\circ h).

(A.14)

b)

Let
\alpha\in(0,2)
,
\beta\in\mathbbm{R}
, and
\gamma\in[0,1)
. Then the bilinear maps

\operatorname{com}_{2}(f,g):=f\prec g-f\prec\!\!\!\prec g.

(A.15)

\operatorname{com}_{3}(f,g):=\left[\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt,f\prec\!\!\!\prec\right]g:=\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997pt(f\prec\!\!\!\prec g)-f\prec\!\!\!\prec\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997ptg.

(A.16)

have the bounds

t^{\gamma}\|\operatorname{com}_{2}(f,g)(t)\|_{\alpha+\beta}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{t}}\|g(t)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}},\qquad t>0.

(A.17)

as well as

t^{\gamma}\|\operatorname{com}_{3}(f,g)(t)\|_{\alpha+\beta-2}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.63885pt^{\gamma,\alpha}_{t}}\|g(t)\|_{\mathscr{C}\hskip 0.81949pt^{\beta}},\qquad t>0.

(A.18)

Proofs can be found in
[
7
]
.

A.3
Estimation of finite-chaos diagrams

In this section we recall a few well-known results on the estimation of finite
chaos diagrams. For additional details see Chapter 10
of
[
8
]
and
[
18
]
. First
of all we need to characterize the local behaviour of the heat kernel
P_{t}(x)
and of the covariance
C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)
of the Gaussian field

Y_{\varepsilon}
.

Remark A.8
.

We sometimes use a slightly different version of the heat kernel, namely

\check{P}_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}}e^{-t}\mathbbm{1}_{t\geqslant 0}

in order to have that
X(t,x)=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{\mathbbm{T}^{3}}P_{t-s}(x-y)v(s,y)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}y
is the stationary solution to

\mathscr{L}\hskip 1.99997ptX=-X+v
. However, the bounds on the covariance
C_{\varepsilon}

remain trivially valid in this setting.

Lemma A.9
.

The heat kernel
P(\zeta):=P(t,x)=\frac{1}{(4\pi t)^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4t}}\mathbbm{I}_{t\geqslant 0}

has the bound

|P(\zeta)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{-3}.

Let
k\in\mathbbm{N}^{4}
a multi-index with
|k|=2k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots+k_{4}
. Then for every multi-index
|k|\leqslant 2
we have:

|D^{k}P_{t}(x)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{-3-|k|}.

Proof.

|P_{t}(x)|(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{3}\lesssim[1+(|x||t|^{-1/2})^{3}]e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{4|t|}}=(1+|\alpha|^{3})e^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{4}}\char 60\relax+\infty

In the same way we prove that
|\partial_{t}P_{t}(x)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{5}
,
|\partial_{x_{i}}P_{t}(x)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{4}
and
|\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}P_{t}(x)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{5}
.

We recall a special case of Lemma 10.14 of
[
8
]
, which
is enough for our purpose. We use the notation
\interleave\zeta\interleave:=(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)
for
\zeta=(t,x)\in\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3}
.

Lemma A.10
.

Let
f,g:\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3}\setminus\{0\}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
smooth, integrable at infinity and such that

|f(\zeta)|\lesssim\interleave\zeta\interleave^{\alpha}
and
|g(\zeta)|\lesssim\interleave\zeta\interleave^{\beta}
in a ball
B=\{\zeta\in\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3}:\interleave\zeta\interleave\char 60\relax 1,\zeta\neq 0\}
. Then if
\alpha,\beta\in(-5,0)
and
\alpha+\beta+5\char 60\relax 0
we have

|f\ast g(\zeta)|\lesssim\interleave\zeta\interleave^{\alpha+\beta+5}

in a ball centered in the origin.

Moreover, if
\alpha,\beta\in(-5,0)
and
0\char 60\relax\alpha+\beta+5\char 60\relax 1

and for every multi-index
|k|\leqslant 2
we have
|\mathrm{D}^{k}f(\zeta)|\lesssim\interleave\zeta\interleave^{\alpha-|k|}
and
|\mathrm{D}^{k}g(\zeta)|\lesssim\interleave\zeta\interleave^{\beta-|k|}
, then

|f\ast g(\zeta)-f\ast g(0)|\lesssim\interleave\zeta\interleave^{\alpha+\beta+5}

in a ball centered in the origin.

Remark A.11
.

The covariance
C_{\varepsilon}
of

Y_{\varepsilon}
can be written as
C_{\varepsilon}=\check{P}\ast\check{C}_{\varepsilon}\ast\check{P}
with
\check{C}_{\varepsilon}(t,x):=\mathbbm{E}(\eta_{\varepsilon}(t,x)\eta_{\varepsilon}(0,0))
. Recall from the introduction that
\check{C}_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\varepsilon^{-5}\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-1}x)
where
\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}
is the covariance of
the Gaussian process
\eta
defined on
\mathbbm{R}\times(\mathbbm{T}/\varepsilon)^{3}
, and
\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}(t-s,x-y)=\Sigma(t-s,x-y)
if
\operatorname{dist}(x,y)\leqslant 1
and
0
otherwise (so that
the family of functions
\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}
is bounded uniformly on

\varepsilon
by a
C^{\infty}_{c}
function). Then there exists a family of
functions
C_{Y}^{\varepsilon}
defined on
\mathbbm{R}\times(\mathbbm{T}/\varepsilon)^{3}
such that
C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\varepsilon^{-1}C_{Y}^{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{-2}t,\varepsilon^{-1}x)
and

C_{Y}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=[\check{P}\ast\check{C}_{\varepsilon}\ast\check{P}](t,x)
.

Lemma A.12
.

The covariance
C_{\varepsilon}

has the bound, for every multi-index
|k|\leqslant 2
:

|D^{k}C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{-1-|k|}.

Moreover, we have

\varepsilon^{|k|+1}|D^{k}C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|\lesssim 1

Proof.

The first bound is obtained directly from Lemma
A.9
and
Lemma
A.10
. Indeed, since by hypothesis

\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}
has compact support, it is easy to see that
|\check{C}_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|\lesssim(|t|^{1/2}+|x|)^{-5}
.
The second bound is obtained by a simple change of variables in the
convolution defining
C_{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma A.13
.

We have
\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}\lesssim|\log\varepsilon|
and for every
n\geqslant 3

\varepsilon^{n-2}\int_{s,x}P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{n}\lesssim 1
.

Proof.

From the fact that
P_{\varepsilon^{2}s}(\varepsilon x)=\varepsilon^{-3}P_{s}(x)
together with Remark
A.11
we obtain

\int_{\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3}}P_{s}(x)[C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\int_{B(0,\varepsilon^{-1})}P_{s}(x)[C_{Y}^{\varepsilon}(s,x)]^{2}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}x\lesssim|\log\varepsilon|

with
B(0,R)=\{\zeta\in\mathbbm{R}^{4}:\interleave\zeta\interleave\char 60\relax R,\zeta\neq 0\}
a “parabolic” ball centered in the origin. The second
estimation is obtained in the same way.

Lemma A.14
.

For
m\in(0,3)
,
n\in(3,5)
, define
for
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{T}^{3}

I_{m}:=\int|C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta-\zeta^{\prime})|^{m}|\mu_{q,\zeta}\mu_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|,\qquad\tilde{I}_{n}:=\int|C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta-\zeta^{\prime})|^{n}|\tilde{\mu}_{q,\zeta}\tilde{\mu}_{q,\zeta^{\prime}}|

with
\mu_{q,\zeta}:=K_{q,\bar{x}}(y)\delta(t-s)\mathrm{d}\zeta

and
\tilde{\mu}_{q,\zeta}:=\left[\int_{x}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)P_{t-s}(x-y)\right]d\zeta
for
\zeta=(s,y)
. Then

I_{m}\lesssim 2^{mq}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tilde{I}_{n}\lesssim 2^{(n-4)q}.

Proof.

The estimation of
I_{m}
is easily obtained by Lemma
A.12
and a
change of variables. For
\tilde{I}_{n}
observe that for every
q>0

\tilde{\mu}_{q,\zeta}=[\int_{x}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)(P_{t-s}(x-y)-P_{t-s}(\bar{x}-y))]d\zeta

and then we can apply Lemma
A.10
to obtain the result.

Lemma A.15
.

We have for every
\sigma\in[0,1]

\sup_{x\in\mathbbm{T}^{3}}|C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)-C_{\varepsilon}(0,x)|\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1-2\sigma}|t|^{\sigma}

Proof.

It is easy to obtain by interpolation knowing that
|\partial_{t}C_{\varepsilon}(t,x)|\lesssim\varepsilon^{-3}
from
Lemma
A.12
.

Lemma A.16
.

We have for every
\alpha\char 60\relax 3

\sum_{i\sim j}\left|\int K_{i}(x-y)P_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}y\right|\int\frac{|K_{j}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{(|x|+t^{1/2})^{3+\alpha}}

Proof.

We will show that

\left|\int K_{i}(x-y)P_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}y\right|\lesssim 2^{-i}(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-4},

(A.19)

and that

\int\frac{|K_{i}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-\alpha},

(A.20)

from which we deduce that

\sum_{i\sim j}\left|\int K_{i}(x-y)P_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}y\right|\int\frac{|K_{j}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\sum_{i}\frac{2^{-i}}{(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{4+\alpha}}.

Bounding the sum over
i
with an integral, we conclude

\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{\lambda}\frac{\lambda}{(|x|+t^{1/2}+\lambda)^{4+\alpha}}=\frac{1}{(|x|+t^{1/2})^{3+\alpha}}\int_{0}^{1/(|x|+t^{1/2})}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{(1+\lambda)^{4+\alpha}}\lesssim\frac{1}{(|x|+t^{1/2})^{3+\alpha}}.

Let us show (
A.19
). We want to estimate

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int K_{i}(x-y)P_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}y=\int K_{i}(x-y)[P_{t}(y)-P_{t}(x)]\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\int K_{i}(x-y)[P_{t}^{\prime}(x+\tau(y-x))(y-x)]\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle|I|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\int|(y-x)K_{i}(x-y)||P_{t}^{\prime}(x+\tau(y-x))|\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{-i}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\int|yK_{1}(y)||P_{t}^{\prime}(x+\tau 2^{-i}y)|\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-i}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\int|yK_{1}(y)|\frac{e^{-c|x+\tau 2^{-i}y|^{2}/t}}{t^{2}}\mathrm{d}y

where

|P^{\prime}_{t}(z)|=\left|C\frac{e^{-|z|^{2}/t}}{t^{4/2}}\frac{z}{t^{1/2}}\right|\leqslant C\frac{e^{-c|z|^{2}/t}}{t^{2}}.

When
t^{1/2}\geqslant 2^{-i},|x|
we have

|I|\lesssim 2^{-i}t^{-2}\lesssim 2^{-i}(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-4}.

When
2^{-i}\geqslant t^{1/2},|x|
we estimate simply

|I|\lesssim\int|K_{i}(x-y)|P_{t}(y)\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{3i}\lesssim 2^{-i}(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-4}.

When
|x|\geqslant 2^{-i},t^{1/2}
we have instead that either
|x|\geqslant 2\tau 2^{-i}|y|
or
|x|\char 60\relax 2\tau 2^{-i}|y|
. In the
first region
|x+\tau 2^{-i}y|\geqslant c|x|
so

|I|\lesssim 2^{-i}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\int|yK_{1}(y)|\frac{e^{-c^{\prime}|x|^{2}/t}}{t^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{-i}\frac{e^{-c^{\prime}|x|^{2}/t}}{t^{2}}\lesssim 2^{-i}|x|^{-4}\lesssim 2^{-i}(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-4}.

while in the second region
|y|\geqslant 2^{i}|x|/(2\tau)
, then
|yK_{1}(y)|\leqslant|yK_{1}(y)|^{1/2}f(2^{i}|x|/(2\tau))
where

f
is a rapidly decreasing function and in this region

\displaystyle|I|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-i}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau f(2^{i}|x|/(2\tau))\int\frac{e^{-c|x+\tau 2^{-i}y|^{2}/t}}{t^{2}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 2^{-i}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau f(2^{i}|x|/(2\tau))\int\frac{e^{-c^{\prime}|\tau 2^{-i}y|^{2}/t}}{t^{3/2}|\tau 2^{-i}y|}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{-i}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau f(2^{i}|x|/(2\tau))\frac{2^{3i}}{\tau^{3}|x|}\int\frac{e^{-c^{\prime}|y|^{2}/t}}{t^{3/2}}\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-i}}{|x|^{4}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau f(2^{i}|x|/(2\tau))\frac{2^{3i}|x|^{3}}{\tau^{3}}\lesssim\frac{2^{-i}}{|x|^{4}}\lesssim 2^{-i}(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-4}.

So we conclude that (
A.19
) holds. Let us turn
to (
A.20
). When
t^{1/2}\geqslant 2^{-i},|x|
we have

\int\frac{|K_{i}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}\int|K_{i}(x-y)|\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{\alpha}}\lesssim(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-\alpha}.

When
2^{-i}\geqslant t^{1/2},|x|
we estimate

\int\frac{|K_{i}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{\alpha i}\int\frac{|K_{1}(y)|}{|2^{i}x+y|^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{2i}\sup_{z}\int\frac{|K_{1}(y)|}{|z+y|^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{2i}\lesssim(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-\alpha},

and finally when
|x|\geqslant 2^{-i},t^{1/2}
we have either
|x|\geqslant 2^{-i+1}|y|
or
|x|\char 60\relax 2^{-i+1}|y|
. In the first
region
|x+2^{-i}y|\geqslant c|x|
so

\int\frac{|K_{i}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim\int\frac{|K_{1}(y)|}{|x+2^{-i}y|^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim|x|^{-\alpha}\lesssim(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-\alpha},

while in the second
|y|\geqslant 2^{i}|x|/2
, then
|K_{1}(y)|\leqslant|K_{1}(y)|^{1/2}f(2^{i}|x|/2)
where
f
is another
rapidly decreasing function and in this region

\int\frac{|K_{i}(x-y)|}{(|y|+t^{1/2})^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim f(2^{i}|x|/2)\int\frac{|K_{1}(y)|^{1/2}}{|2^{-i}y|^{\alpha}}\mathrm{d}y\lesssim 2^{\alpha i}f(2^{i}|x|/2)\lesssim|x|^{-\alpha}\lesssim(|x|+t^{1/2}+2^{-i})^{-\alpha},

concluding our argument.

Lemma A.17
.

For
m,n\in(0,5)
,
k,\ell\in[0,2)

define

I_{k,m,n}:=\int_{\zeta_{1,2},\zeta^{\prime}_{1,2}}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{k}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{\ell}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta^{\prime}_{1})^{m}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{2}-\zeta^{\prime}_{2})^{n}|\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\mu_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|,

with
\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}
for
\bar{\zeta}=(t,\bar{x})
,
\zeta_{i}=(s_{i},x_{i})

i=1,2
defined as

\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}:=[\int_{x,y}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)\sum_{i\sim j}K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(y-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}.

If
\ell=0
,
0\char 60\relax m+k-2\char 60\relax 5
,
m+k-2\in(-1,5)
and
k+m+n-4\in(0,5)
we have the bound

I_{k,m,n}\lesssim 2^{(k+m+n-4)q}.

If
(k+m-2),(\ell+m-2)\in(0,5)
,
k+m+\ell-4\in(0,5)

and
k+\ell+m+n-4\in(0,5)
we have the bound

I_{k,m,n}\lesssim 2^{(k+\ell+m+n-4)q}.

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\mu_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\sum_{i\sim j}\int_{x,y}K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2})K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})(P_{t-s_{1}}(y-x_{1})-P_{t-s_{1}}(\bar{x}-x_{1}))]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle+[\sum_{i\sim j}\int_{x,y}(K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)-K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2}))K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(y-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2})[P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{2}-x_{1})-P_{t-s_{1}}(\bar{x}-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle+[\sum_{i\sim j}\int_{x,y}[K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)-K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2})]K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(y-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}+\hat{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}

where in the first line we used
\int_{y}K_{i,x}(y)=0
and the fact that

\int_{x}K_{i,x}(x_{1}^{\prime})K_{j,x}(x_{2})=0
if
|i-j|>1
and
\sum_{i,j}K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})=\delta(x_{2}-y)\delta(x_{2}-x)
. Now the
estimation of the term

\bar{I}_{k,m,n}:=\int_{\zeta_{1,2},\zeta^{\prime}_{1,2}}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{k}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}^{\prime}-\zeta^{\prime}_{2})^{\ell}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta^{\prime}_{1})^{m}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{n}|\bar{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\bar{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|,

with
\bar{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2})[P_{t-s_{1}}(x_{2}-x_{1})-P_{t-s_{1}}(\bar{x}-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}
can be done with Lemma
A.10
and gives the
expected result. The integral

\hat{I}_{k,m,n}:=\int_{\zeta_{1,2},\zeta^{\prime}_{1,2}}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{k}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}^{\prime}-\zeta^{\prime}_{2})^{\ell}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta^{\prime}_{1})^{m}C_{\varepsilon}(\zeta_{1}-\zeta_{2})^{n}|\hat{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}||\hat{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1}^{\prime},\zeta_{2}^{\prime}}|,

with
\hat{\mu}_{q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}}=[\sum_{i\sim j}\int_{x,y}[K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)-K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2})]K_{i,x}(y)K_{j,x}(x_{2})P_{t-s_{1}}(y-x_{1})]\delta(t-s_{2})\mathrm{d}\zeta_{1}\mathrm{d}\zeta_{2}
can be estimated
by multiple changes of variables. We have
K_{q,\bar{x}}(x)-K_{q,\bar{x}}(x_{2})=2^{3q}(x_{2}-x)\int_{0}^{1}K^{\prime}(2^{q}(x_{2}-x)\tau-2^{q}(\bar{x}-x_{2}))\mathrm{d}\tau
, and by the scaling properties of

C_{\varepsilon}
and
P_{t,y}
, namely
C_{\varepsilon}(2^{-2i}s,2^{-i}x)\lesssim 2^{i}C_{\varepsilon}(s,x)
and
P_{2^{-2i}s}(2^{-i}x)\lesssim 2^{3i}P_{s}(x)
given by Lemma
A.9
and
Lemma
A.12
, we obtain easily the bound on
\hat{I}_{k,m,n}

by rescaling the integral.

Appendix B
Some Malliavin calculus

We recall here some tools from Malliavin calculus that are widely used in the
rest of the paper. An introduction to Malliavin calculus and the proofs of
some results of this Appendix can be found
in
[
20
,
19
,
24
]
.
Lemma
B.7
was inspired by the calculations
of
[
25
]
.

B.1
Notation

Let
\{W(h)\}_{h\in H}
be an isonormal Gaussian process indexed by a real
separable Hilbert space
H
. Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{P})
a
probability space with
\mathcal{F}
generated by the isonormal Gaussian
process
W
, we note
L^{2}(\Omega)=\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}\mathcal{H}_{n}

the well-known Wiener chaos decomposition of
L^{2}(\Omega)
. For any real
separable Hilbert space
V
and
k\in\mathbbm{N}
, let
\mathrm{D}^{k}:L^{2}(\Omega;V)\rightarrow L^{2}(\Omega;H^{\odot k}\otimes V)
be the
Malliavin derivative and
\delta^{k}:L^{2}(\Omega;H^{\otimes k}\otimes V)\rightarrow L^{2}(\Omega;V)
the divergence operator (also called Skorohod
integral) defined as the adjoint of
\mathrm{D}^{k}
. For
p\geqslant 1
we will
write
\mathbbm{D}^{k,p}(V)\subset L^{p}(\Omega;V)
for the closure of
smooth random variables with respect to the norm

\|\Psi\|_{\mathbbm{D}^{k,p}(V)}=[\mathbbm{E}(\|\Psi\|_{V}^{p})+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbbm{E}(\|\mathrm{D}^{j}\Psi\|^{p}_{H^{\otimes j}\otimes V})]^{1/p}

with the notation
\mathbbm{D}^{k,p}:=\mathbbm{D}^{k,p}(\mathbbm{R})
. Let
\{P_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbbm{R}^{+}}
the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck
semigroup and
L:L^{2}(\Omega)\rightarrow L^{2}(\Omega)
its generator (i.e.

e^{tL}=P_{t}
). Following
[
24
]
we introduce the
Green operator

G_{j}=(j-L)^{-1}

with the notation

G_{[j]}^{[m]}:=\prod_{k=j}^{m}G_{k}\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leqslant j\leqslant m

(B.1)

so that
G_{[j]}^{[j]}=G_{j}
. To avoid confusion, it is worth stressing that

G_{[j]}^{[m]}
is
not
the
m
-th power of the operator
G_{j}
but
just a shortcut for
\prod_{k=j}^{m}G_{k}
.

B.2
Partial chaos expansion

Let
\Psi\in L^{2}(\Omega)
which has the Wiener chaos decomposition
\Psi=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}J_{n}\Psi
. Then by Proposition 1.2.2 of

[
20
]

\mathrm{D}J_{n}\Psi=J_{n-1}\mathrm{D}\Psi
, and knowing
that
LJ_{n}\Psi=-nJ_{n}\Psi
we obtain the commutation property

\mathrm{D}(j-L)^{-\alpha}\Psi=\mathrm{D}\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\frac{1}{(j+n)^{\alpha}}J_{n}\Psi=\sum_{n\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{(j+n)^{\alpha}}J_{n-1}\mathrm{D}\Psi=(j+1-L)^{-\alpha}\mathrm{D}\Psi

(B.2)

for every
\alpha>0
,
j>0
. The above formula holds also for
j=0
if

\mathbbm{E}(\Psi)=0
.

The results we have recalled so far let us write an
n
th-order Wiener chaos
expansion for a random variable in
\mathbbm{D}^{n,2}
:

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\Psi\in\mathbbm{D}^{n,2}
and
G_{[j]}^{[m]}
as
in (
B.1
). Then for every
n\in\mathbbm{N}\backslash\{0\}

G_{[1]}^{[n]}\mathrm{D}^{n}\Psi\in\operatorname{Dom}\delta^{n}
,
J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi\in\operatorname{Dom}\delta^{k}

\forall 0\leqslant k\char 60\relax n
and

\delta^{n}G_{[1]}^{[n]}\mathrm{D}^{n}\Psi=(\operatorname{id}-J_{0}-\ldots-J_{n-1})\Psi=\Psi-\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{k!}\delta^{k}J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi.

(B.3)

Proof.

We have for any
\Psi\in L^{2}(\Omega)
, since
L=-\delta\mathrm{D}

(
[
20
]
, Proposition 1.4.3):

\Psi-\mathbbm{E}(\Psi)=LL^{-1}(\Psi-J_{0}\Psi)=-\delta\mathrm{D}L^{-1}(\Psi-J_{0}\Psi)=\delta(1-L)^{-1}\mathrm{D}\Psi

where we used (
B.2
), and the fact that
(1-L)^{-1}\mathrm{D}\Psi\in\operatorname{Dom}\delta
is obvious by construction. This yields the first
order expansion
\Psi=\mathbbm{E}(\Psi)+\delta(1-L)^{-1}\mathrm{D}\Psi
. Iterating the expansion up to order
n
we obtain
(
B.3
). It is clear that
J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi\in\operatorname{Dom}\delta^{k}
since
J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi
is constant with values in
H^{\otimes k}
. The second equality comes from the fact that
\delta^{k}J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi\in\mathcal{H}_{k}

\forall k\in\mathbbm{N}
, indeed
\forall\Psi^{\prime}\in\mathbbm{D}^{k,2}
:

\mathbbm{E}(\delta^{k}(J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi)J_{k}\Psi^{\prime})=\langle J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi,J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi^{\prime}\rangle_{L^{2}(\Omega;H^{\otimes k})}=\mathbbm{E}(\delta^{k}(J_{0}\mathrm{D}^{k}\Psi)\Psi^{\prime})

In order to obtain
L^{p}
estimations of the remainder term
\delta^{n}G_{[1]}^{[n]}\mathrm{D}^{n}\Psi
generated by expansion (
B.3
), we
used the following lemmas:

Lemma B.2
(
[
20
]
, Prop. 1.5.7).

Let
V
be a real separable Hilbert space. For every
p>1
and every
q\in\mathbbm{N},k\geqslant q
and every
\Psi\in\mathbbm{D}^{k,p}(H^{q}\otimes V)
we have

\|\delta^{q}(\Psi)\|_{\mathbbm{D}^{k-q,p}(V)}\lesssim_{k,p}\|\Psi\|_{\mathbbm{D}^{k,p}(H^{q}\otimes V)}

Remark B.3
.

Let
V
be a real separable Hilbert space. For
every
v\in V
and every
\Psi\in\mathbbm{D}^{q,2}(H^{\otimes q})
with

q\in\mathbbm{N}
we have
\Psi\otimes v\in\operatorname{Dom}\delta^{q}
and

\delta^{q}(\Psi)v=\delta^{q}(\Psi\otimes v).

Indeed, notice that for every smooth
\Psi^{\prime}\in\mathbbm{D}^{q,2}(V)
and
every smooth
\Psi\in\mathbbm{D}^{q,2}(H^{\otimes q})
we have

\mathbbm{E}(\langle\delta^{q}(\Psi\otimes v),\Psi^{\prime}\rangle_{V})=\mathbbm{E}(\langle\Psi\otimes v,\mathrm{D}^{q}\Psi^{\prime}\rangle_{H^{\otimes q}\otimes V})=\mathbbm{E}(\langle\delta^{q}(\Psi)v,\Psi^{\prime}\rangle_{V}).

Now since
\mathrm{D}^{q}(\Psi\otimes v)=\mathrm{D}^{q}\Psi\otimes v
and
\Psi\in\mathbbm{D}^{q,2}(H^{\otimes q})
, we have
\Psi\otimes v\in\mathbbm{D}^{q,2}(H^{\otimes q}\otimes V)
. Lemma
B.2

yields the bound
\|\delta^{q}(\Psi\otimes v)\|_{L^{2}(V)}\lesssim\|\Psi\otimes v\|_{\mathbbm{D}^{q,2}(H^{\otimes q}\otimes V)}
which allows to
pass to the limit for
\Psi^{\prime}
and
\delta^{q}(\Psi\otimes v)
in
L^{2}(V)
.

Lemma B.4
(
[
24
]
, Prop. 4.3).

For every
j>0
the operator
(j-L)^{-1/2}
is bounded in
L^{p}
for
every
1\leqslant p\char 60\relax\infty
.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
j\in\mathbbm{N}\backslash\{0\}
and
V
a
real separable Hilbert space. There exists a finite constant
c_{p}
such that
for every
\Psi\in L^{p}(\Omega,V)
:

\|\mathrm{D}(j-L)^{-1/2}\Psi\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,H\otimes V)}\leqslant c_{p}\|\Psi\|_{L^{p}(\Omega,V)}

(where the operator
\mathrm{D}(j-L)^{-1/2}
is defined on every
\Psi

which is polynomial in
W(h_{1}),\ldots,W(h_{n})
and can be extended by
density on
L^{p}
).

Proof.

First notice that we can suppose w.l.o.g.
\mathbbm{E}(\Psi)=0
